The first `unknown` now defers the deadline exactly once (bounded escalation; a second one does not defer), and the App feeds the shared reconnect ledger into the planner so a deferred L2 no longer silences the guard for a backoff window.
- **Logs never write through a symlink** — both the control-plane and the Swift sidecar refuse a symlinked leaf/directory (the guard used to run only in the constructor, while `start()`'s unconditional reopen re-activated a degraded sink); modes are re-applied on every open (0700/0600 are create-time only); external deletion self-heals in a bounded sweep; a blocked rotation degrades to stdout instead of zeroing the watermark and growing the file ~2x per cycle.
- **The failure overlay removes the veil from the DOM** — its buttons used to stay focusable and announced behind it; the queue note no longer sits under `aria-busy`; a failed veil switch reports honestly instead of leaving a sticky abandonment mark.
- **The liveness boundary takes a watermark at decisive settlement** — late or out-of-order settlements no longer drag an already-advanced failure verdict back, and same-tick boundary cases are decided correctly.
- **The reaper no longer claims an unverified legacy token** — taking over a legacy token now requires the entry's own dsh identity to be recognizable; otherwise the record is kept and marked identity-unverified, so another process is never killed by mistake.
- **CSP style sources are evaluated through the effective chain** — effective style sources follow the CSP3 fallback chain (`style-src-elem` -> `style-src` -> `default-src`) with case-insensitive nonce/hash handling, so the injected root rule cannot be fooled by a style-src declared elsewhere.
- **The cross-language lockstep tests can no longer be fooled by comments** — both assembly locks matched raw source, so commenting out the apply/install call stayed green; they now strip comment lines before matching.
- **The packaged mobile composer is no longer covered by the keyboard** — the old compensation inferred the keyboard from `innerHeight` and the visual viewport and wrote the offset twice (as the seat's sticky bottom *and* as a scrollport padding); because the scrollport is also the seat's sticky containing block that overshot by the full offset (measured 368px, with a missed event leaving the composer covered by 336px). The guard now measures the conversation scrollport's border-box overlap — a value invariant under its own writes, so the verification loop has a fixed point — writes only the frame custom property plus an in-flow spacer for the scroll range, and carries a 96/72px hysteresis, a bounded re-measure (≤2 steps) and the `data-mobile-kbd-state` diagnosis; a carrier that answers a write with movement is latched and reported instead of chased.
- **The Git worktree removal dialog no longer contradicts itself** — the risk confirmation claimed the branch was unaffected while the same flow offers a separate "also delete the local branch" checkbox; it now states only what will be discarded and leaves the branch to that checkbox.

- **Blank icons in the settings nav, the sidebar wordmark and the settings seat are fixed (design 05 §4.2)** — the same symptom survived the entrance-animation retirement; the real-machine cause is that upstream icons hard-code their Figma export ids while `url(#...)` resolves **document-wide**: an N-ctx document defines the same id once per instance shell, and macOS WKWebView drops and caches the whole shape when a newly painted icon resolves a clipper/mask inside a hidden shell (attribute writes, revealing the shell and view transitions do not heal it). A renderer-side scoper installed before the React root now makes every `<svg>` self-contained: ids both defined and referenced inside one `<svg>` are renamed to a document-unique token, definitions referenced from another svg are copied into the consumer, and ids referenced by aria attributes, inline `<style>` or document stylesheets keep their names. The upstream fix is filed as a proposal.
- **The boot veil is no longer painted through by the tenant, and it lifts as soon as the session surface is drawn (design 05 §2.2.1/§4)** — two real-machine shapes: during the hold the official composer (`position: sticky`, z-index 7) painted above the z-index 1 veil ("an input bar inside a blank screen"), and a reveal gate fed only by two asynchronous App-side mirrors held the veil over an already-rendered shell until the open budget burned out ("blank screen / loading shown directly", alternating). The stacking boundary now sits at the tenant root (`.instance-shell{isolation:isolate}`), the tenant is hidden for the hold (folded into the hidden-shell no-animation gate), and the reveal is decided by the `[data-phase]` the shell itself exposes (`active` immediately, `absent` with a 2s fallback, `hero`/`settling` held with a 70s outer insurance; release is a level, bound to the request identity), with a hard cut only for switches whose landing surface is the veil, so an old view's input bar cannot blend with the new veil.

- **A remote session open no longer parks on the loading hint** — the opening budget's widening key moves from "request" to "logical-stream episode" (a rebuilt stream no longer inherits up to 300 s of widening, and the ledger is bounded) and the keep-alive race re-schedules on the microtask queue; the retry lane's wait for a live connection generation is capped at 30 s and resolves as expired so the caller reopens instead of throwing; the teardown path runs the same silent-carrier escalation but requires the stream to have lived at least 15 s (a session switch, an aborted request or a remount no longer churns a healthy carrier); the open-in ladder auto-rebuilds only a stall whose `openPromise` is exactly null and whose state is loading, adds a 90 s hard-failure copy ("the session content was not loaded"), and leaves the manual rebuild ungated by the ledger.
- **Silent completion: a running bit stuck true lost the completion edge** — the official store's running bit comes from a single, never-retransmitted mux event, so one lost frame means forever-running and a completion notification that never arms; a single authority chain now reconciles it (an independent unary `session.list` read, N=2 agreement, written back through the official public path), giving every running episode exactly one completion edge; the thresholds (probe 60 s / reconnect 190 s and only with stuck evidence / notice 310 s) are single-sourced in the shared tables, and every action lands in the bounded on-machine ring `dsh-chamber.authority-log.v1` for later reading.
- **Completion unread and notifications no longer vanish when a shell is reclaimed** — the hidden-shell channel is no longer the only completion edge: the gateway mirror and the shell-less observers are consumed (an SSH or local source observes the instance-proxy mux's waterfall frames without ever settling an approval on another client's behalf), the ledger survives a reclaim, and a background completion keeps lighting the sidebar dot, the todo strip, the app badge and the notification.
- **Facts from different incarnations were merged into one contradictory view** — the source registry is keyed by `sourceId+epoch`: one id has exactly one live generation, `reincarnate()` is the only new-generation entry, a dispatch for a superseded epoch is dropped, and projections read only the current entry.
- **The veil was held to the outer bound and re-armed at 0 ms** — the reveal frame now carries an absolute deadline: `hero`/`settling` past 70 s becomes actionable (the veil lifts and the exit actions stay), `absent`/an unknown phase falls back after 2 s, an unknown `data-phase` is no longer folded into hero and parked until 70 s, and a bad clock returns Infinity (hold) instead of re-arming at 0 ms.
- **A switched source's first frame flashed the previous source's theme; a truncated effect teardown** — the theme is cached per source fingerprint from settled snapshots (LRU 12), so an unmounted new source prefers its own cache and a cold switch no longer flashes the previous source's ground; the truncated effect cleanup is restored.
- **A stale subagent count was presented as "running" and suppressed unread** — rows gain `subagentActivity: none|running|unknown`: an absent lineage index is unknown (not none), a stale report downgrades running to unknown, and unknown renders neutrally — no more "N subagents running" fiction, and no more swallowed unread.
- **A projection delay was folded into "not connected"** — a missing source phase now publishes `unknown` (the sidebar reads "unknown") instead of idle, so hover and aria no longer describe a delayed projection or a failed fetch as a manual disconnect.
- **An unreadable runtime failure ledger was treated as "no failures"** — the gateway status gains `failureError` and the version list gains `removableVersionsError`: a read failure emits one line ("the runtime failure ledger is unreadable…; the failure record may be incomplete") and forces `removableVersions` to project empty (the cleanup row hides and the cleanup guard refuses at acceptance).
- **Audit P0 (client face)** — the local card's "clean up and take over" shares the same runtime spawn gate as "start" (unsafe phases such as hydrating/applying fail closed), so a spawn can no longer slip through a pending switch window; the facts SSE silence watchdog arms when the request is issued and gains a connect deadline, so a half-dead tunnel is no longer both un-stale and unreconnecting.
- **Managed-host spawn cwd incident (design 02 §3.1)** — the installed layout no longer uses the in-place-replaceable install tree as cwd (which left the host holding an unlinked directory, so `worker_threads`' shared `process.cwd()` failed every tool call with `uv_cwd ENOENT`); it uses the control plane's own 0700 `<stateDir>/dsh-home` instead, while the source layout keeps the workspace root.
- **Three fail-closed gaps on the audit face** — (1) any remote plugin change must read the pre-change journal snapshot first and is refused outright when that read fails (otherwise a later undo deletes an in-place-upgraded plugin instead of restoring it); (2) the activation probe's expected domain set is derived after the spawn snapshot; (3) `openInApp/probe` actually joins the probe registry and is judged by the `{ok,value.platform}` shape, so a missing open-in host domain is detectable.
- **Runtime readability fails closed** — a metadata read no longer folds EACCES/EIO into corrupt/missing: it classifies once as `present/missing/corrupt/unknown` and the store, snapshot and health projection branch on all four; the gateway status projects `failureError` instead of a fabricated zero, and the boot gate treats `metadataRecoveryPending() === "unknown"` as blocking; an unreadable leaf is no longer quarantined, overwritten or reported as empty.
- **The gateway session-mux grace timer re-arms when it fires inside its window** — the timer callback could observe `graceMs - 1` and reject the sweep, and that rejection was final until the next 5 s tick: with no further event a held waterfall never delegated at all (reproduced under CPU load and in CI). It now re-arms for the remaining window.
- **Desktop notifications key on completion identity, plus badge and held-resume flushes** — the dedupe claim takes the content watermark as its fifth component (two hits of one completion collapse into one banner while a later completion of the same session is a new event), the renderer's merged unread count is projected onto the app badge, and a held system-resume flushes as soon as the renderer is deliverable again.
- **The macOS shell no longer treats a probe error as healthy, and renderer crashes are attributable** — the hang watchdog gains `noteProbeFailed()` (an erroring probe is a strike, the strike limit reloads once, the first give-up alerts while later ones only log, and a successful `didFinish` resets the gate); the WebContent crash callback records "X.XXs since the last finished load (boot window vs stable) + crash N within that load" and follows it with a "reloaded X.XXs after the crash" line, so a silent whole-page reload finally leaves a post-mortem.
- **Boot-window rAF hot paths caused WebContent crashes** — the sidebar scroll-sync's container-missing retry is frame-tight only for 200 ms and then falls back to the 80 ms timer cadence, and the anchored row is resolved with one escaped attribute lookup instead of a full row scan; InstanceView's surface sampling is coalesced through a frame coalescer (first change on the next frame, bounded interval, guaranteed tail sample), removing the boot-window "sample every frame → commit React every frame" hot shape.
- **The test suite drops fixed ports and leaked timers** — the sidecar fixture uses `--port 0` and reports the OS-assigned port in its ready frame, the manager-api's consecutive-port reservation retries only on EADDRINUSE, and watchdog/grace timers are cleaned up in a finally; the cleanup-deadline injection seam applies to dev/test only and is ignored in an assembled build.
- **The root typecheck program is no longer polluted by the chamber client plugins** — the root tsconfig maps the four `/client` specifiers to the renderer's local consumer face while each plugin package's own program stays authoritative (the previous union pulled four loose vendor faces into one program and produced 117 errors); the vendor dependencies wrongly added to layout, mobile, open-in and renderer are removed.
- **The scoper marker gate no longer reds every real build, and the committed mobile bundle matches its source** — the marker carried by a bare call that minification removed is replaced with an anchored assignment checked for a real call (the negative control still holds), and the guard now covers the committed mobile bundle, the post-build page chunks, the bytes copied into the Swift app and both release legs; the committed mobile bundle that disagreed with its source is fixed alongside.
- **CLI `connections add/reclaim` no longer fabricate a contract-legal null** — a `connection:null` answer prints `connection none` and a missing `spawned` prints `unknown`, instead of inventing a `local` row with `spawned:false`.
- **Failure copy and hostile values** — OpenIn's failure reasons all go through the shared `describeThrown` (never throws, never empty; an empty message falls back to the name and then to "unknown error"); Git gains ten localized failure copies (operation in progress / recover first / facts unavailable / worktree outside the topology / not linked to a workspace / worktree unavailable / archive failed / refresh failed / Git plugin not loaded / remote call failed), and only a genuinely unmapped failure keeps its raw English message.

## [0.3.2-beta.6] - 2026-09-23

### Added
- **A local source's missing graph endpoint now reports the cause, not only its effect** — a new boot-gap fact, `local-graph-not-injected` (local instances only; remote and gateway behaviour stays verbatim): when the local instance never injected chamber's client-plugin graph channel, the banner names that cause (installation/seed integrity) first and outranks the `required-services-missing` consequence that arrives 5s later; it is revocable and re-checked within the same ready generation (one bounded +30s re-check; the fact is cleared as soon as the provider arrives), so a stale banner no longer burns a cold re-mount.
- **The diagnostic names the missing provider** — the console diagnostic line goes from naming only the waiter (ui-chat) to naming the missing provider as well (`sidebarRight → @deepseek-ai/dsh-client-ui-sidebar-right`); a service with no registered provider row says "provider row unknown", and a missing row is no longer zero-log.
- **The Windows leg now runs the frontend contracts, and the release proof demands the Windows packaging rehearsal** — ci.yml's Windows leg gains the renderer and sidebar `test:win32` manifests (the root `test:win32` fans out to five packages, each a separately named step), every manifest carrying a "zero tests = failure" guard; `verify-release-ci-proof.mjs`'s `REQUIRED_JOB_STEPS` requires both steps plus the Windows packaging rehearsal, so deleting or renaming one turns red.
- **A denied notification is no longer a dead end (design 19 §3.3/§4.1)** — `dsh-chamber:notify` resolves `{ shown, error? }` instead of a boolean: the host/OS refusal reason and the adjudication-side suppression reasons survive the IPC boundary; a new `dsh-chamber:open-notification-settings` invoke (fixed main-side URL, never a renderer-supplied one) lets the settings page render the reason, a permission hint and an "Open System Settings" button (zh/en), with the pure mapping unit-tested; the Swift shell logs authorization decisions and delivery failures into native-shell.log.
- **A conversation stream-health arm (design 14 §D4)** — the api-gateway stream carrier retries instead of failing terminally and projects a page-level `stream-carrier-failed` fact; the open-in plugin renders the session stream-health chip seat; the control plane logs the abandoned upgrade for attribution; a heal judged failed now **latches** the reload action instead of waiting out the whole rolling budget (the judgment hangs off the settle clock, not the phase, so a `loading` dwell or a hidden stretch cannot swallow it), and carrier churn is published to the surface instead of staying inside the seat closure. The 2026-09 freeze investigation closes the loop: the opening frame is validated against a replaced or closing socket (a silent RFC 6455 discard now counts as a carrier failure), a logical stream's first item gets a 30s deadline (relaxed up to 4x while timeouts stay consecutive), the WebSocket handshake gets 30s, a lost socket re-queues its own reconnect (from 1s, capped at 10s; a reconnect ordered by the connection pump does not count as a failure), a journal silent for 45s probes a sibling follow and replaces the physical generation only when the Host cursor actually advanced, and the health chip gains a **user-triggered** "Rebuild stream" control (capability guard, arm-then-single-execution, cooldown shared with the automatic heal).
- **macOS Swift native shell (design 25 route A, preview)** — macOS gains a second shell: Swift/AppKit implements only the shell (window, WKWebView, menu/notifications/badge/deep link/dialogs/external open/hide-restore) and carries **no business logic**; the business runs unchanged as a packaged Node sidecar executable (the existing control-plane plus the desktop package's pure-Node business modules) reached over a trusted stdio JSON-RPC channel, while the page side gets an injected shim equivalent to the preload in place of `window.dshChamber`. The web UI is reused 100%. It **coexists** with the Electron build: artifacts are `dsh-chamber-<version>-macos-arm64.dmg/.zip` (this release inverts the naming, see Changed), the bundle id is `com.dshchamber.native` (its own notification-authorization identity), and both flavors share one userData root and directory lock (`<userData>/.dsh-chamber.lock`, darwin `flock`/`O_EXLOCK`; the exclusive lock itself is the only arbitration authority), and the close decision only ever reads the **current generation's** close-context facts: cancelling a quit no longer leaves the next close on the old verdict, while a settings change and a sidecar restart each invalidate once and a restart pre-warms one decision so the ms-level close path survives.
- **The native flavor's in-app update chain (design 25 §7, D-1 = B)** — now Sparkle 2: the shell checks a real EdDSA-signed appcast (public/private keys come from the release chain), supports in-app download, install and relaunch, and keeps the manual "Check for Updates…" entry; the beta channel's rolling appcast carries both the current beta and the latest final so beta clients can see final too (S-22/S-23/S-36). An unavailable update surface, a bad feed/key, or a busy-state click returns an honest error instead of being swallowed (S-37–S-39).
- **Anti-drift lockstep for both flavors, plus a new CI leg** — independent gates now cover the IPC-surface mirror (literal channel strings and structures on both the main and preload sides), the bridge manifest (`bridge-manifest.json` ↔ the generated Swift whitelist, 68 channels = 60 invoke + 8 push), the injected shim surface, the electron-free transitive closure of the core, and the packaging manifest with artifact names (`-native` names never collide, and update-feed ownership is unique); a new macOS CI leg `test-macos` (Swift build + XCTest + packaging dry runs + the darwin lock and packaging-script suites) joins the release proof, which now requires the linux, windows and macos legs to pass together.
- **Packaging chain** — `build:sidecar` (the official Node archive is verified against a SHA-256 pinned in the repository before bundling, and the executable must keep the basename `node`; the bundled dsh workspace and embedded pnpm ride along) and `build:swift-app` (assemble → ad-hoc or Developer ID signature → notarize → staple → archive, fail-closed on any missing input) publish in parallel with the Electron artifacts under one tag without overwriting them.
- **A local log file for the native shell (design 25 diagnostics, T-25)** — the native shell previously left no local dump to post-mortem after a double-click blank window or quit; key lines (launch, sidecar spawn/exit, navigation failure, update phases, quit chain) are now also written to `<userData>/logs/native-shell.log` with a 256 KiB single rotation (`.1`) at 0600/0700, falling back silently to stdout if the file cannot be opened. It deliberately does **not** reuse Electron's `<userData>/state/host-logs/<port>.log` — that is the control plane's per-port managed-host stdout/stderr pipe; the two log surfaces live in different directories and must not be conflated; the wake chain and every shell-to-page renderer push (failure branches included) go through this log too, so a field report can separate "never sent" from "never consumed".
- **Electron mac packaging rehearsal on the push path (G41)** — the `test-macos` leg on main pushes now runs an ad-hoc, `--publish=never`, credential-free/notarization-free/upload-free `electron-builder --mac --arm64` rehearsal followed by `verify-electron-artifacts.mjs` against the real `.app`; the release proof (`verify-release-ci-proof.mjs`'s `REQUIRED_JOB_STEPS`) requires that exact step name, so deleting or renaming it turns red. The cost is a full packaging run on every push's macos leg (a longer leg), traded for exposing files/extraResources/beforePack/afterPack/entitlements breakage on push instead of waiting for the release run (draft already created, credentials loaded).
- **Fail-closed gates on the update chain (G42)** — in a formal release, a Sparkle public key present without the private key now FAILs (the shell would poll a feed nobody signs); a private key present with the beta/stable appcast missing FAILs (no more silently skipping the rolling publish); a non-404 `/releases/latest` parse failure or a final with a native zip that fails to download FAILs; the new `verify-native-appcast.mjs` asserts the current version's appcast entry carries `shortVersionString`=this version, `sparkle:version`=this `.app`'s CFBundleVersion, and an enclosure pointing at this version's zip (run both in the appcast step and the rolling refresh). Both keys absent stays a loud downgrade (artifacts still ship, clients just cannot see the update); a repository with no final release yet, or a latest final that genuinely has no native zip, keeps a loud warning.
- **The native shell follows the display refresh rate (S-48)** — the native flavor used to cap rendering near 60Hz, which felt visibly duller than Electron on a ProMotion/high-refresh panel. It now follows the display it is on (halved in low-power mode), applies the preference before the web view is constructed, and reads it back before mutating; measured at 114-120fps in the native shell.
- **Viewport elastic overscroll is gone in the native shell (S-50)** — macOS WebKit bounces at the viewport layer: with the pointer over non-scrollable chrome (title bar, session-list header), or after a scroller hits its end, the whole page (including `position: fixed` layers) shifted down and sprang back. The shell injects one root-level `overscroll-behavior: none !important` rule at documentStart, main frame only, without touching any scroller's own scroll semantics.
- **Control-plane and sidecar diagnostics are persisted too (T-25 extension)** — continuing the native shell's local log, the control-plane log and the sidecar diagnostics now land on disk at 0600: a double-click failure is no longer guesswork from the system log.
- **The boot dead zone for a not-ready source is escapable (design 05 §4.1)** — opening a session on a remote source that was not ready used to sit on a full-window veil for up to the 135s harvest arm with no navigation exit. The veil now acts per phase: an idle (not connected) source immediately offers Connect plus row switching and does NOT start the boot; `error` and managed `stopped`/`restart-exhausted` fail fast after a 1.5s grace (no longer waiting the full 60s); `degraded` (reconnect in flight) is not declared dead and keeps waiting in budget; a hung boot past the feedback window (10s) offers retry/connect/switch plus the Cmd-R hint; a 502 (tunnel up, remote port dead) is a non-blocking banner that self-heals once per ready generation.
- **A stale session running-bit self-heals** — when a session had been judged finished while its running bit never settled, the chat surface used to stay unmounted; the sidebar session facts and the renderer liveness guard now reconcile it in three tiers and the chat surface mounts again.
- **Login-phase pre-warm in the gateway (design 17 §10.6)** — while an unauthenticated visitor types the password the login page prefetches the **real** `/plugins` bundle URLs (the HTTP cache is URL-keyed, so a wrapped URL buys nothing), and the first post-login screen is served from that cache instead of paying the measured ~4.35 MiB gzip download on the critical path; the capability travels as a short-lived HttpOnly cookie (HMAC, bound to the client address, 120 s). The route is consulted before the auth gate but claims only the roster's two real bundle shapes with a valid capability — an absent/expired/tampered/foreign cookie falls back to the prior 401/session verdict and its category-only audit, every other `/plugins/**` path stays closed, rate and capacity are fail-closed (429/503, and the limiter is only consulted for an already-verified capability) — and `--no-warmup` / `DSH_GATEWAY_WARMUP=0` turns it off (the login page HTML then returns byte-identically to the old template).

- **Native-shell copy is localized and follows the in-app language (design 25 §5.3)** — the native shell's menu, tray, dialogs and error copy used to be hard-coded Chinese; the whole table (121 keys) now ships as en / zh-Hans string resources, with the key set locked to the single `NativeTextKey` source and every placeholder identical in kind and order. Shell-authored copy follows the **page language** (an in-app switch takes effect immediately) while system and framework surfaces (Sparkle's standard window, AppKit built-ins, the context menu) follow the system language by default, and `AppleLanguages` is written only when the language family differs, so those surfaces follow too (next launch). Assembly asserts existence, byte equality and `plutil` for every `.lproj`, so a missing resource no longer ships.

- **A shared session-lifecycle package, plus three lockstep gates (design 14 §D4)** — a new dependency-free, clockless state package, `@dsh-chamber/dsh-stream-state`: the carrier-rebuild reducers and throttle tables, the page-lifecycle ledgers/containers, the reveal arbiter, ONE threshold table for all four recovery ladders, bounded async primitives (`withDeadline`/`waitForCondition`), the load state machine with its generation fence and the reconcile authority decision, plus Foundation-only Swift mirrors (`CarrierDecision.swift`/`LoadState.swift`). The api-gateway, renderer, sidebar, mobile and open-in all consume this one owner, so each threshold exists once; three lockstep gates ride static, CI and release validation (the differential replay fails on drift≠0, the Swift mirror is compile-asserted, and the ladder tables are compared module by module), and the release adds a **bounded stream-forensics ring** (256 entries, FIFO, redacted at record time, written before the live dispatch) plus a page-side snapshot bridge, so carrier decisions can be read back after the fact even in the Swift shell, which has no DevTools.
- **The remote session-state mirror and completion unread (design 17 §10.7, design 06, design 19)** — the control plane gains the read-only session-state protocol and one mux observer per host (a reconnect always reconciles a full baseline, a held waterfall is never answered on another client's behalf, and it only observes `$events` — it never settles an approval); the gateway serves `GET /chamber/session-state`, `/stream` (SSE with `Last-Event-ID` resume or a snapshot fallback), `POST /read` and `/read-all` (on by default; `--no-session-state`/`DSH_GATEWAY_SESSION_STATE=0` turns each route into 503), stores metadata only at 0600/0700, and treats corrupt state as non-empty rather than empty. The client side upgrades "completion unread" from a hidden-shell channel to an independent fact channel: the renderer derives unread from facts plus a per-install read mark, projects notifications from the five-tuple identity, and keeps the ledger after a shell is reclaimed (a background completion still lights the sidebar completion dot, the todo strip, the app badge and the notification); the sidebar gains machine-readable row markers and "Mark all read", and every in-app session creation carries its origin; the phone reports its read watermark as it reads (one persisted client id per install, monotonic, 5 s throttled, silent on failure), so reading on the phone clears the desktop dot and badge.
- **Signed delta updates for the native shell (design 25 §7)** — Sparkle moves from "download the whole archive every time" to real deltas: per-channel appcast staging (grouped by the archive's embedded `SUFeedURL`; a mixed directory fails with `multiple appcasts found`), only baselines with a non-empty `SUPublicEDKey` equal to the signing key and the same branch point are accepted, every baseline is accounted for through `sparkle:deltaFrom`/`.ignore`, `verify-native-appcast` **verifies** the Ed25519 signature of the released zip and of every delta, the rolling beta feed merges from the published stable feed, re-running the same version is allowed only when its zip is byte-identical to the published one, and the appcast is uploaded after the archives.
- **Gateway plugin undo restores instead of removing (design 21 §6.3/§6.8)** — a new `POST /chamber/plugins/undo` (202 `{accepted,opId}` plus tasks polling) takes the latest successful op carrying a preImage, pair-checks the backup's package.json + lockfile and restores lockfile first, manifest second; the undo writes its own journal entry (`kind:'undo'` + `undoOf`) after backing up the current state, so it can be undone again; the read face localizes 409 `runtime_busy` / 503 `write_fence_unavailable`, and the plugin dialog no longer renders the "change log" area.
- **A single-writer state-root lease at `<stateRoot>/owner.json` (design 17 §12, design 25 §6.3)** — no-follow O_EXCL plus a read-back final check; a live pid is refused with the machine-readable `state_root_locked` and the holder's pid/flavor; a dead pid is claimed by rename with byte/inode proof; the gateway, desktop, CLI, standalone and sidecar all adopt it, and **a second writer for one state root fails closed with exit 1**; resolution is single-sourced in `resolveStateRoot` (`--state-dir` > `DSH_<FLAVOR>_STATE` > `DSH_CHAMBER_STATE` > `~/.dsh-chamber`), and the legacy `.gateway.lock`/`dsh-runtime/owner.json` are retired once.
- **Single-sourcing and named package boundaries (design 01 §5, design 21 §6.11, design 24 §3)** — three new packages: `@dsh-chamber/dsh-chamber-wire` (host↔client wire contracts: the plugin manifest and masking, the `PluginRow` row model, archive-cleanup, runtime-status), `@dsh-chamber/dsh-chamber-client-core` (the browser-safe shared client core that replaces `sidebar/src/shared`) and `@dsh-chamber/dsh-stream-state`; eleven packages drop their `./src/*` wildcard exports and consumers move to explicit named faces (all 782 runtime exports have a production consumer). On the host side: the Electron main and the Swift sidecar share one `host-assembly` (assembly duplication 167 → 3 lines; `main.ts` 4097 → 1391, `shell-core.ts` 4560 → 2492); the gateway runtime splits into `src/runtime/*` (runtime-manager 3179 → 551 lines with the same exported API and a self-contained dist); `App.tsx` is decomposed into app-hooks; dsh-runtime gets an explicit entry surface. A zero-tolerance package-boundary gate (no cross-package relative imports in production code; the exports whitelist consistent in both directions) and C16 join the gates.
- **Build-time artifacts no longer live in git** — nine bundles (dsh-runtime's dist, the four seeds' dist, mobile's dist+lib) leave the index and their build becomes an explicit prerequisite (`build:artifacts`/`ensure:artifacts`, one manifest source); every check mode either bootstraps or fails loudly (naming the id, path and build command; static stays read-only and never dirties the tree), and `verify-artifact-freshness` covers four "stale = red" classes: the mobile artifacts, the compiled preload, the renderer's generated files and the gateway bundle.
- **One pooled test runner** — each package resolves its manifest directly with `DSH_TEST_MANIFEST_DUMP=1` (no pnpm startup), and every entry runs on ONE bounded global pool with round-robin scheduling across steps (transcripts still flush in declaration order); `--jobs`/`DSH_TEST_JOBS` is validated before anything runs, a missing manifest dump is a hard failure instead of a silent pass, and node/tsc steps no longer go through pnpm; `run-checks.mjs <mode> --list` prints the plan.
- **A wider release-validation leg** — the validation job gains the remote-state fault-injection matrix and the acceptance matrix (blocked is not green, an empty group is an error), the three stream-state lockstep gates and the package-boundary/dead-export/upstream-lifecycle-contract gates; a release commit must first have a fully successful run on `main` across the linux, windows and macos legs.

### Changed
- **win32 private-state I/O no longer requires `O_NOFOLLOW`** — on a platform without the flag it falls back to identity re-verification: lstat before and after open rejects symlinks and re-checks dev/ino, failing closed when unprovable (the mutation transaction no longer throws on its first step); on Windows an existing `<userData>/dsh-runtime` is no longer misread as corrupt and blocked forever. Recorded as design 23 F8 (the identity fallback's residual TOCTOU).
- **win32 plugin packaging launches pnpm through node** — Windows no longer spawns `pnpm.cmd` directly (Node ≥ 20.12 rejects `.cmd`) but runs `node <pnpm.cjs>`, while POSIX keeps the bare `pnpm`; `resolvePnpmBinDir` gains the win32 candidates and the bundled pnpm.
- **The packaging closure is fail-closed** — afterPack asserts on every platform that the packaged runtime tree carries `ui-sidebar-right` / `client-resources` / `ui-chat`, and startup re-samples the executable tree loudly; a runtime missing them can no longer ship.
- **The taskbar badge toggle is capability-gated** — `supported.badgeSupported` is false on win32 and the settings page disables the toggle with the reason shown (it used to be an unexplained no-op switch).
- **The native shell's page path costs less main-thread serialization** — `writeJSONString` goes from a per-scalar build to a single UTF-8 scan (about 10.9x on pure ASCII, still 2-5x on escape-dense input) and the envelope gate takes a sound upper bound first, falling back to the exact measurement only at the boundary (1 MB envelope: 3.01 ms -> 1.57 ms), with byte-identical output.
- **Documentation corrections** — the README pair's userData path is now `%APPDATA%\@dsh-chamber\desktop`; the DEVELOPMENT pair's user-visible Windows packaging target is nsis only; the packaging-closure counts are corrected.
- **The desktop main process is split into an electron-free core plus two host-edge implementations** — the orchestration and business logic of `main.ts` moved into Electron-independent `shell-core.ts`, the Electron native edges stay in `electron-edges.ts`, and the Node/sidecar edges live in `node-edges.ts`; the Electron build behaves identically (only four files genuinely import electron, asserted by the transitive-closure gate) while the native flavor reuses the same business code.
- **`dsh-chamber:info` and the host-facts surface expose a flavor discriminator** (pages can branch on the flavor), and host facts (focus / window shown / system resume) are pushed from one source on both sides.
- **The macOS support floor is raised to 14.4 (S-30)** — Electron's `build.mac.minimumSystemVersion` and the native shell's `LSMinimumSystemVersion` both declare the exact 14.4, and `Package.swift` declares `.macOS(.v14)` (SwiftPM can only name a major). Why: the native shell runs the OS WebKit, and the shipped bundle calls `Promise.withResolvers` directly on the approval-decision, user-question/plan-review and PDF-preview construction paths; that API first exists in Safari 17.4 / macOS 14.4, so 13.x and 14.0–14.3 throw a construction-time `TypeError`. Electron's bundled V8 is unaffected, but one support matrix keeps a single floor (no polyfill).
- **The native shell window geometry narrows toward Electron (S-49)** — the height gap between the two flavors is narrowed to an explicit compromise (1280x786), registered as a reviewable deviation row.
- **Tighter write side and frame ceiling on the protocol** — sidecar outbound frames are capped and the protocol writer is bounded: an oversized response fails closed, settles every pending request and reports loudly instead of leaving a renderer request hanging forever or letting a buffer grow without bound.
- **Lower hot-path cost in the native shell (performance)** — repeated work and per-frame allocations are gone from the hot path; the native shell measures 114-120fps.
- **Release naming inverted: the Swift native shell is now `dsh-chamber`, Electron is `dsh-chamber-electron`** — the native leg drops the `-native` suffix from its .app/DMG/zip/volume names (bare `dsh-chamber`), and the Electron leg gains `-electron` on its app, installer and artifact names. The appId, the native CFBundleIdentifier, the shared userData identity (`@dsh-chamber/desktop`), the directory lock and the `dsh-chamber` deep-link scheme are unchanged, so permissions, credentials and the two-flavor coexistence semantics are unaffected; an already-installed old .app keeps its directory name (an update replaces the running bundle), so a clean folder name needs a reinstall.

- **Public entry faces narrowed (BREAKING)** — the `./src/*` wildcard exports are gone repo-wide and a package root's `.` now points at source (source-only); consumers (including anyone using this repo as a plugin or an embedder) must switch to the explicit named faces; `@dsh-chamber/dsh-runtime`'s entry becomes an explicit named face (137 runtime values plus the types their signatures reference) with internals reachable only by in-package relative imports; the host primitives (pnpm entry, anchor version, dsh CLI entry, registry URL, version safety) move into dsh-runtime and are adopted by the gateway.
- **The desktop IPC surface shrinks 69 → 68 (invoke 61 → 60)** — the whole-table SSH-instance write channel `desktop_ssh_instances_set` is removed end to end; connection changes are only the transactional save/delete plus three single-credential setters that accept clear only; `bridge-manifest.json` stays locked to the Swift whitelist.
- **The local plugin-manifest masking gap is closed (design 21 decision 18)** — the desktop's `LOCAL_PLUGIN_LIST` no longer passes local paths through: both `dependencies` and `rows[].spec` go through `redactLocalPluginManifest`, with the criterion single-sourced in `dsh-chamber-wire`.
- **One shared restricted plugin-mutation executor (design 21 §6.3)** — the control-plane executor becomes the only plugin-mutation subprocess implementation for the gateway and the desktop main process: an env allowlist (dropping `DSH_GATEWAY_*`/`npm_config_*`/`NODE_AUTH_TOKEN`), POSIX process-group leadership with a timeout group-kill, bounded output truncation, and error text redacted by the caller's own function before it reaches a journal or a projection.
- **Packaging shape and self-containment assertions** — the desktop's `build-control-plane` becomes a single self-contained esbuild bundle (absolute entry, node22 ESM, require shim) with a new "the rebuilt artifact may only have itself as an input" assertion (a residual specifier fails the build); the gateway bundle is rebuilt self-contained; the `electron-builder` `files` closure follows.
- **macOS Swift single-sourcing** — new `PrivateFS.swift` (the criterion union for no-follow private-file opens: regular file, single hard link, O_NOFOLLOW, fstat dev/ino agreement, size ceiling), `RollingWindowLimiter.swift` (one limiter decision shared by the external-open budget, the sidecar restart backoff and the renderer reload), `StrictJSONNumber.swift` (strict JSON number reads; an `NSNumber as? Bool` misread is banned) and `BridgeLimits.swift` (one 4 MiB budget for both bridges); the criteria only tighten.
- **Installer input validation converges** — `install-gateway.sh`'s port/canonical SemVer/bind/origin/IPv4/version comparisons become one embedded pure-node program (with its own tests); a missing node now fails the validation outright while the corrected copy stays unchanged.
- **Contract docs and the notices file** — design-document status lines lose their dates and "implemented" narratives; design 17 §10.7/§12, design 21's undo route and fence, design 18 §7's artifact discipline, design 02 §3.1 and design 25 §6.3/§7 are added or rewritten, and eleven design documents gain Rejected alternatives; the third-party notices are regenerated for the current tree (38 packages).

### Fixed
- **The win32 liveness probe no longer requires the English `LISTENING`** — a `Get-NetTCPConnection -State Listen` JSON probe (with a unit-tested pure parser) plus a case-insensitive netstat fallback share one 500ms cache, so a live port is no longer judged dead on a non-English Windows.
- **Residual descendants are identity-checked before a bare-PID kill** — the kill re-verifies ProcessId + CreationDate/CommandLine against the same CIM table, skips on a mismatch and fails closed when unprovable; `kill(pid, 0)` is no longer treated as liveness evidence on win32.
- **The icacls verification is no longer always false** — flags are parsed as a token set (the real `(OI)(CI)(F)` and the `/grant`-shaped legacy rendering land in the same set), the ACE tail is anchored on its last `:` so a path with spaces no longer pollutes the principal, and inherited ACEs, Everyone/Users/SYSTEM and DENY all fail, restoring the check's discriminating power.
- **The win32 installer's publish/backup renames go through the retry path** — sharing `renameWithWindowsRetry` with the runtime, so a transient lock no longer rolls back the whole transaction.
- **A missing seed artifact is no longer skipped silently** — a source directory present without `dist/index.js` now names the id, package, path and consequence (a packaged host entry logs at error), while an all-missing seed still clears the overlay and returns null.
- **An unverifiable running bit is no longer kept indefinitely** — once fact reads keep failing past the bound, only the running assertions are dropped (rows and groups stay, and nothing re-archives) and the source is handed to the existing session-stall banner ("cannot confirm session state"), with the next successful read (push or unary) restoring facts immediately; the sidebar's stale remote running bits are cleared by a local verdict (a local read plus an independent unary authority read, written back through upstream's public `handleSessionStatus(id, false)`), so no upstream patch is needed.
- **The chat surface and sidebar seats stay visible under stream churn** — four `from-opacity:0` entrance animations could freeze on their first frame inside a hidden or occluded N-ctx shell, leaving the HARNESS mark and the settings gear invisible while still clickable; they are retired and the hidden-shell gate is tightened alongside.
- **A server-option press survives WebKit in the settings page** — WKWebView does not focus a button on mouse-down, so the dropdown's focused search input blurred (`relatedTarget: null`), the shell's onBlur closed the portal and the click that would switch servers never reached the unmounted row; suppressing the mousedown default keeps the focus (and the row) alive until the click activates it, the same device upstream's MenuView uses, with keyboard Enter, outside pointerdown, mask and Escape semantics unchanged.
- **The native dmg carries the drag-to-install cue** — the native dmg used to hold only the app plus the /Applications symlink, so Finder opened a plain window; `macos/scripts/dmg.mjs` now builds one UDZO image with electron-builder's background and icon coordinates and self-verifies it by **content**: it reads `.DS_Store` and asserts the background type, the alias pointing at the in-volume background, the icon size, the window bounds and both icon coordinates, so a cue-less or mis-placed dmg can no longer ship.
- **Immediate reconnect and held delivery after system wake never fired in the native flavor** — the wake notification was registered on the wrong notification center; it now registers with `NSWorkspace.shared.notificationCenter`.
- **keep-awake no longer prevents display sleep** — aligned with the Electron `prevent-app-suspension` semantics (and design 14 D5): system sleep only.
- **Confirmation dialogs no longer show their body twice** (when a call site passes the same copy as both title and message, the native shell rendered it in both places).
- **A sidecar restart no longer drops buffered, unsent deep links** — reset only clears the readiness bit; the buffer survives and is delivered FIFO on the next ready frame.
- **An oversized response no longer leaves a renderer request hanging forever** — a response past the frame limit now fails closed, settling every pending request and reporting loudly.
- **Quit cleanup explicitly releases keep-awake and clears the Dock badge.**
- **A packaged .app ignores `POC_*` environment overrides** — these dev/POC switches used to stay live in an assembled bundle, letting the environment redirect the shell to any node, sidecar script, web dist, userData or control-plane origin; the dev path (`swift run` / non-.app) is unaffected.
- **A local false red in the new test gate, and a `build-sidecar` defect exposed by the first CI run** — the desktop test-manifest lockstep used to scan `packages/desktop/.dev-user-data` (another checkout left behind by dev runtime state) and fail `check:tests` locally, and is now ignored; `build-sidecar`'s "no bundled dsh workspace" warning branch threw `TypeError` on a clean checkout because the injected io lacked `warn`, and now falls back to the console.
- **Native-shell download destination aligned with Electron (S-26)** — Swift used to show an NSSavePanel (cancellable) while Electron has no `will-download`/`setSavePath` anywhere and takes Chromium's silent default into `~/Downloads`; Swift now writes silently through `DownloadDestination`: the directory is created when missing, duplicate names are deduplicated with Chromium's ` (n)` rule, in-flight paths are reserved so a batch cannot collide, and a resolution/creation failure honestly cancels and records the reason (never silently changing destination). Known small deviation (accepted): WebKit has no `.crdownload` intermediate state, so a crash mid-download can leave a partial file under its final name (Electron leaves `.crdownload`, which never masquerades as a finished file).
- **Native-shell page zoom survives relaunch (T-22)** — `WKWebView.pageZoom` used to live only in the instance and reset to 100% on every launch; it is now stored per origin in UserDefaults (restored at setup, written by zoomIn/zoomOut/reset, with bad values normalized and clamped), matching Electron's per-origin persistence; the storage medium is still each flavor's own preference store and is not shared across flavors.
- **Packaged Chinese locale resources are no longer silently deleted (S-47)** — the mac leg's `electronLanguages` now uses Electron's real directory spelling `["en","zh_CN"]` (app-builder-lib matches exactly or by prefix, so `"zh-CN"` can never match `zh_CN.lproj` and the Chinese `locale.pak` was dropped, falling Chromium-level strings back to English; the top-level hyphenated value stays for the win/linux `.pak` legs); afterPack and the artifact gate assert on the real `.app` that every declared locale is present with a non-empty `locale.pak`.
- **A single transient unknown verdict no longer burns the spell's only L2 reconnect** — with the outcome window (150s) below the coalesce window (200s), one transient probe failure was read as a definitive failure and fired the only L2 reconnect of that spell. The first `unknown` now defers the deadline exactly once (bounded escalation; a second one does not defer), and the App feeds the shared reconnect ledger into the planner so a deferred L2 no longer silences the guard for a backoff window.
- **Logs never write through a symlink** — both the control-plane and the Swift sidecar refuse a symlinked leaf/directory (the guard used to run only in the constructor, while `start()`'s unconditional reopen re-activated a degraded sink); modes are re-applied on every open (0700/0600 are create-time only); external deletion self-heals in a bounded sweep; a blocked rotation degrades to stdout instead of zeroing the watermark and growing the file ~2x per cycle.
- **The failure overlay removes the veil from the DOM** — its buttons used to stay focusable and announced behind it; the queue note no longer sits under `aria-busy`; a failed veil switch reports honestly instead of leaving a sticky abandonment mark.
- **The liveness boundary takes a watermark at decisive settlement** — late or out-of-order settlements no longer drag an already-advanced failure verdict back, and same-tick boundary cases are decided correctly.
- **The reaper no longer claims an unverified legacy token** — taking over a legacy token now requires the entry's own dsh identity to be recognizable; otherwise the record is kept and marked identity-unverified, so another process is never killed by mistake.
- **CSP style sources are evaluated through the effective chain** — effective style sources follow the CSP3 fallback chain (`style-src-elem` -> `style-src` -> `default-src`) with case-insensitive nonce/hash handling, so the injected root rule cannot be fooled by a style-src declared elsewhere.
- **The cross-language lockstep tests can no longer be fooled by comments** — both assembly locks matched raw source, so commenting out the apply/install call stayed green; they now strip comment lines before matching.
- **The packaged mobile composer is no longer covered by the keyboard** — the old compensation inferred the keyboard from `innerHeight` and the visual viewport and wrote the offset twice (as the seat's sticky bottom *and* as a scrollport padding); because the scrollport is also the seat's sticky containing block that overshot by the full offset (measured 368px, with a missed event leaving the composer covered by 336px). The guard now measures the conversation scrollport's border-box overlap — a value invariant under its own writes, so the verification loop has a fixed point — writes only the frame custom property plus an in-flow spacer for the scroll range, and carries a 96/72px hysteresis, a bounded re-measure (≤2 steps) and the `data-mobile-kbd-state` diagnosis; a carrier that answers a write with movement is latched and reported instead of chased.
- **The Git worktree removal dialog no longer contradicts itself** — the risk confirmation claimed the branch was unaffected while the same flow offers a separate "also delete the local branch" checkbox; it now states only what will be discarded and leaves the branch to that checkbox.

- **Blank icons in the settings nav, the sidebar wordmark and the settings seat are fixed (design 05 §4.2)** — the same symptom survived the entrance-animation retirement; the real-machine cause is that upstream icons hard-code their Figma export ids while `url(#...)` resolves **document-wide**: an N-ctx document defines the same id once per instance shell, and macOS WKWebView drops and caches the whole shape when a newly painted icon resolves a clipper/mask inside a hidden shell (attribute writes, revealing the shell and view transitions do not heal it). A renderer-side scoper installed before the React root now makes every `<svg>` self-contained: ids both defined and referenced inside one `<svg>` are renamed to a document-unique token, definitions referenced from another svg are copied into the consumer, and ids referenced by aria attributes, inline `<style>` or document stylesheets keep their names. The upstream fix is filed as a proposal.
- **The boot veil is no longer painted through by the tenant, and it lifts as soon as the session surface is drawn (design 05 §2.2.1/§4)** — two real-machine shapes: during the hold the official composer (`position: sticky`, z-index 7) painted above the z-index 1 veil ("an input bar inside a blank screen"), and a reveal gate fed only by two asynchronous App-side mirrors held the veil over an already-rendered shell until the open budget burned out ("blank screen / loading shown directly", alternating). The stacking boundary now sits at the tenant root (`.instance-shell{isolation:isolate}`), the tenant is hidden for the hold (folded into the hidden-shell no-animation gate), and the reveal is decided by the `[data-phase]` the shell itself exposes (`active` immediately, `absent` with a 2s fallback, `hero`/`settling` held with a 70s outer insurance; release is a level, bound to the request identity), with a hard cut only for switches whose landing surface is the veil, so an old view's input bar cannot blend with the new veil.

- **A remote session open no longer parks on the loading hint** — the opening budget's widening key moves from "request" to "logical-stream episode" (a rebuilt stream no longer inherits up to 300 s of widening, and the ledger is bounded) and the keep-alive race re-schedules on the microtask queue; the retry lane's wait for a live connection generation is capped at 30 s and resolves as expired so the caller reopens instead of throwing; the teardown path runs the same silent-carrier escalation but requires the stream to have lived at least 15 s (a session switch, an aborted request or a remount no longer churns a healthy carrier); the open-in ladder auto-rebuilds only a stall whose `openPromise` is exactly null and whose state is loading, adds a 90 s hard-failure copy ("the session content was not loaded"), and leaves the manual rebuild ungated by the ledger.
- **Silent completion: a running bit stuck true lost the completion edge** — the official store's running bit comes from a single, never-retransmitted mux event, so one lost frame means forever-running and a completion notification that never arms; a single authority chain now reconciles it (an independent unary `session.list` read, N=2 agreement, written back through the official public path), giving every running episode exactly one completion edge; the thresholds (probe 60 s / reconnect 190 s and only with stuck evidence / notice 310 s) are single-sourced in the shared tables, and every action lands in the bounded on-machine ring `dsh-chamber.authority-log.v1` for later reading.
- **Completion unread and notifications no longer vanish when a shell is reclaimed** — the hidden-shell channel is no longer the only completion edge: the gateway mirror and the shell-less observers are consumed (an SSH or local source observes the instance-proxy mux's waterfall frames without ever settling an approval on another client's behalf), the ledger survives a reclaim, and a background completion keeps lighting the sidebar dot, the todo strip, the app badge and the notification.
- **Facts from different incarnations were merged into one contradictory view** — the source registry is keyed by `sourceId+epoch`: one id has exactly one live generation, `reincarnate()` is the only new-generation entry, a dispatch for a superseded epoch is dropped, and projections read only the current entry.
- **The veil was held to the outer bound and re-armed at 0 ms** — the reveal frame now carries an absolute deadline: `hero`/`settling` past 70 s becomes actionable (the veil lifts and the exit actions stay), `absent`/an unknown phase falls back after 2 s, an unknown `data-phase` is no longer folded into hero and parked until 70 s, and a bad clock returns Infinity (hold) instead of re-arming at 0 ms.
- **A switched source's first frame flashed the previous source's theme; a truncated effect teardown** — the theme is cached per source fingerprint from settled snapshots (LRU 12), so an unmounted new source prefers its own cache and a cold switch no longer flashes the previous source's ground; the truncated effect cleanup is restored.
- **A stale subagent count was presented as "running" and suppressed unread** — rows gain `subagentActivity: none|running|unknown`: an absent lineage index is unknown (not none), a stale report downgrades running to unknown, and unknown renders neutrally — no more "N subagents running" fiction, and no more swallowed unread.
- **A projection delay was folded into "not connected"** — a missing source phase now publishes `unknown` (the sidebar reads "unknown") instead of idle, so hover and aria no longer describe a delayed projection or a failed fetch as a manual disconnect.
- **An unreadable runtime failure ledger was treated as "no failures"** — the gateway status gains `failureError` and the version list gains `removableVersionsError`: a read failure emits one line ("the runtime failure ledger is unreadable…; the failure record may be incomplete") and forces `removableVersions` to project empty (the cleanup row hides and the cleanup guard refuses at acceptance).
- **Audit P0 (client face)** — the local card's "clean up and take over" shares the same runtime spawn gate as "start" (unsafe phases such as hydrating/applying fail closed), so a spawn can no longer slip through a pending switch window; the facts SSE silence watchdog arms when the request is issued and gains a connect deadline, so a half-dead tunnel is no longer both un-stale and unreconnecting.
- **Managed-host spawn cwd incident (design 02 §3.1)** — the installed layout no longer uses the in-place-replaceable install tree as cwd (which left the host holding an unlinked directory, so `worker_threads`' shared `process.cwd()` failed every tool call with `uv_cwd ENOENT`); it uses the control plane's own 0700 `<stateDir>/dsh-home` instead, while the source layout keeps the workspace root.
- **Three fail-closed gaps on the audit face** — (1) any remote plugin change must read the pre-change journal snapshot first and is refused outright when that read fails (otherwise a later undo deletes an in-place-upgraded plugin instead of restoring it); (2) the activation probe's expected domain set is derived after the spawn snapshot; (3) `openInApp/probe` actually joins the probe registry and is judged by the `{ok,value.platform}` shape, so a missing open-in host domain is detectable.
- **Runtime readability fails closed** — a metadata read no longer folds EACCES/EIO into corrupt/missing: it classifies once as `present/missing/corrupt/unknown` and the store, snapshot and health projection branch on all four; the gateway status projects `failureError` instead of a fabricated zero, and the boot gate treats `metadataRecoveryPending() === "unknown"` as blocking; an unreadable leaf is no longer quarantined, overwritten or reported as empty.
- **The gateway session-mux grace timer re-arms when it fires inside its window** — the timer callback could observe `graceMs - 1` and reject the sweep, and that rejection was final until the next 5 s tick: with no further event a held waterfall never delegated at all (reproduced under CPU load and in CI). It now re-arms for the remaining window.
- **Desktop notifications key on completion identity, plus badge and held-resume flushes** — the dedupe claim takes the content watermark as its fifth component (two hits of one completion collapse into one banner while a later completion of the same session is a new event), the renderer's merged unread count is projected onto the app badge, and a held system-resume flushes as soon as the renderer is deliverable again.
- **The macOS shell no longer treats a probe error as healthy, and renderer crashes are attributable** — the hang watchdog gains `noteProbeFailed()` (an erroring probe is a strike, the strike limit reloads once, the first give-up alerts while later ones only log, and a successful `didFinish` resets the gate); the WebContent crash callback records "X.XXs since the last finished load (boot window vs stable) + crash N within that load" and follows it with a "reloaded X.XXs after the crash" line, so a silent whole-page reload finally leaves a post-mortem.
- **Boot-window rAF hot paths caused WebContent crashes** — the sidebar scroll-sync's container-missing retry is frame-tight only for 200 ms and then falls back to the 80 ms timer cadence, and the anchored row is resolved with one escaped attribute lookup instead of a full row scan; InstanceView's surface sampling is coalesced through a frame coalescer (first change on the next frame, bounded interval, guaranteed tail sample), removing the boot-window "sample every frame → commit React every frame" hot shape.
- **The test suite drops fixed ports and leaked timers** — the sidecar fixture uses `--port 0` and reports the OS-assigned port in its ready frame, the manager-api's consecutive-port reservation retries only on EADDRINUSE, and watchdog/grace timers are cleaned up in a finally; the cleanup-deadline injection seam applies to dev/test only and is ignored in an assembled build.
- **The root typecheck program is no longer polluted by the chamber client plugins** — the root tsconfig maps the four `/client` specifiers to the renderer's local consumer face while each plugin package's own program stays authoritative (the previous union pulled four loose vendor faces into one program and produced 117 errors); the vendor dependencies wrongly added to layout, mobile, open-in and renderer are removed.
- **The scoper marker gate no longer reds every real build, and the committed mobile bundle matches its source** — the marker carried by a bare call that minification removed is replaced with an anchored assignment checked for a real call (the negative control still holds), and the guard now covers the committed mobile bundle, the post-build page chunks, the bytes copied into the Swift app and both release legs; the committed mobile bundle that disagreed with its source is fixed alongside.
- **CLI `connections add/reclaim` no longer fabricate a contract-legal null** — a `connection:null` answer prints `connection none` and a missing `spawned` prints `unknown`, instead of inventing a `local` row with `spawned:false`.
- **Failure copy and hostile values** — OpenIn's failure reasons all go through the shared `describeThrown` (never throws, never empty; an empty message falls back to the name and then to "unknown error"); Git gains ten localized failure copies (operation in progress / recover first / facts unavailable / worktree outside the topology / not linked to a workspace / worktree unavailable / archive failed / refresh failed / Git plugin not loaded / remote call failed), and only a genuinely unmapped failure keeps its raw English message.

## [0.3.1] - 2026-09-15

### Added
- **Opt-in application log bridge for the managed host (design 18 §3.4 diagnostics)** — the managed dsh had no application-logging surface to turn on: the web CLI exposes only `--host/--no-open/--port/--trusted-host`, the runtime installs no exporter at all (`LoggerService` keeps a 1000-entry in-memory ring), and a host-side incident could not be diagnosed from outside the process. `DSH_CHAMBER_HOST_LOG_LEVEL` now opens one: unset/off keeps the profile overlay byte-identical, a level name selects that Cordis threshold, and an unrecognized value mounts at the conservative `warn` while warning loudly (the threshold is monotone — `warn` includes info). The implementation is a chamber-generated, dependency-free Cordis plugin mounted as an ordinary seed entry that writes each message to stderr, where the existing child-output pipeline already redacts and rolls it into `host-logs/<port>.log`; the redaction rule is extracted into a pure function so a test can pin it without spawning a host. It deliberately does **not** join `CHAMBER_HOST_PACKAGES`: it carries no Typert Remote domain, and a registry row without a probe domain would fail the activation gate.
- **Mobile session-open surface reworked (design 17 §18.6)** — ① Session-header crowding (the crumb strip was forced to `flex-wrap: wrap`, letting the unclassed sub-agent count span break inside CJK words into five lines and pushing the row from 30px to ~96px) goes back to the official single line with horizontal panning: the phone tier pins the row to 48px with top/right safe-area insets, states the shrink order (the current crumb gives width up first; the lineage chip does not shrink because it is the only entry to the subagent catalog), keeps a 44px touch floor on the lineage trigger, and makes the two icon seats mean the box (border-box) so they cannot paint past a notch; the new 480px and 360px tiers bound the agent-preset label, where the old `> button > span` rule targeted a node no registrant renders and was dead code. ② Frame stamping is now all-or-nothing: a frame whose conversation column is missing stays unstamped, so the stylesheet's column lock cannot trap the transcript in a 0px first track if the vendor renames the centre slot key, and the drawer toggle/backdrop render only when the sidebar role was actually found (no dead control, no stray scrim) while the probe records what it found in `data-mobile-roles`. ③ **Session-open stall notice**: neither the client nor the host puts a deadline on opening a session, so a stalled stream parks the transcript on "loading history" forever. An attribute-only notice (active/settling conversation root, a chat flow with no anchor row, a rendered session header, sustained for 45s of VISIBLE time) offers a single user-initiated reload and never reloads or reopens anything by itself; the hint's own copy node is never matched.
- **Repository gates, one check entry, and a mobile acceptance toolbox** — three gates no existing check could see (`verify:test-wiring`: every test file on disk must be reachable; `verify:md-links`: relative links and anchors in the owned documentation must resolve; `verify:workflow-yaml-scalars`: a step name such as `single entry: ...` is invalid YAML while every text-based workflow check stays green) plus one `pnpm run check` entry (`scripts/dev/run-checks.mjs` static/tests/typecheck modes, the same entries in CI and locally instead of parallel lists); `release-preflight` now scans `packages/` and `scripts/` for `FIXME` markers and blocks a release unless `--allow-fixme` is passed explicitly, so shipping one is a recorded decision rather than an oversight (`TODO`/`XXX` stay ordinary backlog tags). Acceptance gains a **CDP mobile walkthrough** (`Emulation.setDeviceMetricsOverride` + touch emulation + WebSocket frame capture; the verdict baseline is the device width, because `mobile:true` enables shrink-to-fit and makes `scrollWidth <= innerWidth` tautological; credentials come only from environment variables named on the command line and pass through redaction before any output) and a **mobile plugin anchor freshness gate** (every declared anchor must still be emitted by the pinned upstream, with a 19-item minimum set as the backstop; a missing upstream tree fail-soft-skips and prints what it skipped), and the five core packages (control-plane / dsh-runtime / renderer / gateway / cli) gain contract READMEs.

### Changed
- **The gateway installer unit gets the login environment systemd omits (design 17 §5)** — the default service shape (empty `SERVICE_USER`) renders no `User=` line, and systemd sets `$HOME/$LOGNAME/$SHELL` only for units carrying `User=`/`DynamicUser=`/`PAMName=`; the gateway and every child it spawns (managed dsh → code runtime → bash tool → gh / npm / git credential helper) therefore ran with an EMPTY HOME — gh reported "not logged in", npm could not find its cache, git credential helpers read no token. `write_unit` now injects `HOME/LOGNAME/USER/XDG_CONFIG_HOME` for exactly that shape, taking the values from the RUN user's passwd entry (never the installer's `$HOME`, which sudo may carry over), and an unresolvable home is a hard failure; the `--service-user` shape keeps systemd's own derivation and injects nothing. **Migration**: an in-place deployment must re-run `install` to regenerate the unit (it also regenerates credentials).
- **Proxy compression negotiation (design 17 §8, design 03)** — every proxied request used to lose its `accept-encoding`, forcing the managed host to answer with identity bytes even though the pinned gzip middleware was ready to compress: the official frontend's first load is a single ~10.65 MiB client-module combo (it embeds pdfjs-dist for the document-preview plugin), measured at 4.16 MiB at the configured level 1. Identity is now required only where the proxy itself needs the raw bytes: HTML document navigations (the S0 trust injection prepends a script and recomputes content-length) and `Accept: text/event-stream`; everything else negotiates with the upstream, whose own filter already refuses event-stream and content-range. The same commit lands the narrow `ProxyForwardDeps.onUpstreamResponseHeaders` seam (called before content-length is recomputed, fail-soft) and exports `isHashedStaticAssetPath` for its gateway consumer.
- **The gateway CSP allows the upstream `<base href="/">` element** — `@deepseek-ai/dsh-host-frontend-static` re-injects `<base href="/">` into every rendered index document (its SPA deep-link fix); under `base-uri 'none'` the browser refuses that element outright, so a deep link resolves its own `./assets/...` URLs against the deep-link path, 404s, and paints a blank page. The proxy cannot rewrite the streamed HTML any more than it can backfill the nonce, so `base-uri` follows `script-src` onto the same-origin allowance; every other directive is byte-identical.
- **The gateway caches content-hashed frontend assets immutably** — the official frontend is served by `dsh-host-frontend-static`, which writes only a content type (no Cache-Control/ETag/Last-Modified), so despite Vite naming every file with a content hash a repeat visit could re-download the whole shell. Exactly `/assets/<name>-<8 hex>.(js|css|woff2?|svg)` now gets `cache-control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable` on a plain 200 with no content-range, and the decision is bounded by evidence rather than path alone: no upstream cache-control/etag/expires, no percent-escape or dot-segment path, and a content type the extension can actually produce; `favicon.svg`, `manifest.webmanifest`, `index.html`, `.map` files and any range response never match. The seam runs before the proxy recomputes content-length, so it cannot corrupt framing.
- **Auth-rejection audit records coalesce into windows** — every rejected request used to append an audit line and fsync it; an unauthenticated phone load fires a burst of them (manifest, icons and service worker all fetch without credentials), so a single page view cost several synchronous fsyncs and produced several identical lines that told a reader nothing extra. Records are now windowed by `(client, code, path category)`: the first event is still appended immediately (the evidence that something happened must not wait for a timer), in-window duplicates only increment a counter, and the window's close writes one line carrying `count:<n>` (the window total, including the anchor); different clients, codes or path categories never coalesce. The window state lives in the dispatch closure (not a module singleton) and its timer is `unref`'d so it cannot hold the process open, while `quiesce()` drains every open window before ownership is released; distinct client identities are unbounded, so the window map is capped and publishes the oldest window early at the cap instead of growing forever.
- **The protected set gains a version dimension (design 21 §6.11.1)** — post-install verification judged every hoisted top-level copy against the dsh generation string, so installing the official opt-in layer failed loudly with four findings: its transitive experimental packages are official-scope but absent from F (C11 forbids that segment), and re-scoped vendored packages (cosmokit, schemastery) keep upstream versions that can never equal the generation string. F now carries a **name→version fact** beside the name set, with a second trust criterion that refuses a source whose name and version parsers disagree about the same keys instead of silently losing the version dimension and re-enabling the false positives. Verification runs two arms in a fixed order: a name in F is judged against a version this runtime line provides (callers passing no fact map keep the generation arm, §6.11.4), and a name outside F is exempt only when an OFFICIAL direct dependency's closure reaches it — the walk expands official nodes only, so a third-party intermediary cannot explain an official name. The desktop main process and the gateway executor pass the facts, and the built-in anchor is used only when it IS the active runtime line (an env-provided dev tree may fall back to it so its write face is not degraded).
- **Workspace creation and adoption ride a single fact channel (design 05 §2.2.1, design 08 §4.2)** — the Git worktree plugin created and adopted workspaces through its own unary `workspace.create`, while only the sidebar's add-workspace dialog published the workspace-echo fact; for a source whose shell is not mounted there is no other read channel for a brand-new (0-session) workspace (the unary fallback derives its groups from session cwds, and a source that already pushed keeps its workspace set frozen), so that row appeared only after the user clicked the server. create/delete/rename now go through `shared/workspace-mutations.ts` wrappers, each performing the wire call and publishing the matching one-way fact so no call site can forget it; the Git create carries its placement anchor (the echoed row lands directly below its main checkout instead of at the tail) and decorates BEFORE the fact (`beforePublish` — the worktree flags and the unregistered-block collapse are written in the same synchronous continuation, so the row is born in its final shape and position), and adopt carries its branch title, so a row is born with its final label. Two review findings in the echo rules are fixed with it: anchor placement now groups by anchor with a fresh lookup (the old cursor stored absolute indices, so an interleaved anchor shifted the next row one slot early), and the adopt decoration skips the main checkout (it used to mark the main row as a derived worktree).

### Fixed
- **A newly installed or rebuilt client plugin did not appear after restarting dsh (design 18 §3.6 item 8)** — the window's client-plugin set is fixed at each instance shell boot (the host graph is fetched once per boot and the page module table is first-load-wins per plugin id), so restarting dsh refreshed the host side while the window kept the pre-restart set and a new `dsh.client` contribution (a settings section, for example) only appeared after a full app restart. A **page-owned completion** in the sidebar shared face now ends the action: one entry per source key (`local`/`gateway-<id>`/`dsh-<id>`), single-flight, **not cancelled when the initiating panel unmounts**, never reloading when readiness does not arrive within the page-level net (and reporting that honestly instead of hiding the failure in a fresh boot), and armable again after it settles. Wired to every user-initiated plugin-refresh restart: the dsh-runtime section's restart on both shapes, the local apply-now / retry-apply / retry-restore transactions (only on success), the local card's start and writer takeover, the gateway card's restart/start, the plugin dialog's footer restart and every restart-to-apply path (row remove/add/import/undo/bulk apply), and the ssh card's service restart (dsh targets only); restarting the gateway service (systemd) does not change the instance's plugin set and is deliberately not armed. Design 18 §3.6 item 8 records the contract, the wiring inventory and the rejected alternatives.
- **The gateway no longer reads "could not read" as "there is nothing to keep"** — two write-face paths folded read failures into emptiness, and the consequence was deletion of evidence: ① a profile manifest that exceeded its read bound, sat behind a symlinked parent or was not valid JSON produced an empty referenced set, so every staged `.tgz` was deleted as an orphan — non-ENOENT read failures, invalid JSON and corrupt journals now BLOCK the sweep with a loud warning, and only a genuine ENOENT keeps the previous cleanup semantics; ② a corrupt journal parsed to an empty list, so reconciliation reported "no pending operations carried over", never reaped a recorded child, and the following prune deleted the preImages of the lost operations — corruption is now distinguishable from an empty journal, the original bytes are kept as a `.corrupt-<ts>` aside, pruning keeps every preImage while evidence is unresolved, and a file that cannot be renamed aside is never overwritten.
- **Plugin archive integrity on both the build and the scan side** — ① the ustar name field is 100 BYTES while the builder compared UTF-16 code units, so a CJK path could pass the check and be truncated inside the header while the builder still reported the full name; it now measures the UTF-8 byte length with the boundary pinned at 100 vs 101 bytes. ② the tar reader returned the first parseable manifest in archive order while pnpm installs `package/package.json`, so a crafted archive with a decoy root manifest made the write face judge the wrong package name; the install path is now preferred with a root manifest only as a fallback, and a mismatch between declared and installed identity fails loudly with both names. ③ the gateway scanner left its manifest capture open, so any entry after the manifest was appended to the captured bytes and a valid archive was rejected as `tgz_invalid`, oversized candidates were sticky and the capture had no bound; the capture now closes with the candidate's data area, oversized is per-candidate, and only bounded declared bytes are retained.
