- **Proxy response-header whitelisting and log-bridge unmounting** — ① the `onUpstreamResponseHeaders` seam re-applies `RESPONSE_HEADER_WHITELIST` after the callback, so a callback that writes `content-length`/`transfer-encoding` (or any non-representation header) cannot reach the wire even though the JSDoc claimed framing was untouchable and `content-length` is not whitelisted but only re-derived when the upstream declared one and the response is not SSE; both the chunked and the SSE shape are tested. ② the generated log-bridge exporter is now mounted through the plugin's OWN `ctx.effect`: cordis's `LoggerService.exporter()` registers its effect on the service's context (the app root) and returns that disposer, so a direct registration survived the plugin's unload and a loader remount stacked a second exporter that wrote every application line twice. ③ the immutable cache rule uses the shared `isHashedStaticAssetPath` predicate instead of a bare `/assets/` prefix, which would have pinned a future unhashed entry for a year.
- **The connections live-state cell no longer reports bundle layers as restart-pending** — the cell matched the Loader snapshot by exact module name, but a `dsh.bundle` package is never a Loader row (the composed tree is the empty root entry list plus the insert rows of each bundle's `cordis.patch.yml`), so every bundle layer matched nothing and showed "activates on restart" forever, including after a restart that really did activate it. A row with no same-name Loader entry is now neutral and the dead locale key is gone; the other honest ceilings are untouched (an unreadable snapshot, protected/composition/seed rows, and disabled/failed/loading states keep their own answers, and the manifest is still never a liveness source).
- **The desktop local patched probe derives its answer from both mount sources** — a local chamber row is mounted from two places: the profile's own `cordis.patch.yml` user layer, which app-boot composes over the root entry list, and the `--patch` overlay handed to the spawn. The probe read the overlay file only, so it reported false while the composed tree carried the row, and it kept reporting true from a leftover overlay that this spawn no longer passes. The probe now answers "does this row reach the composed tree" from both sources (an exact insert row in the profile patch, or in the overlay), and unreadable or absent facts stay false rather than being guessed; the producer side keeps the overlay file's presence meaning exactly "the last spawn passed it": every resolution that passes no overlay deletes the leftover file, and the overlay resolver is exported so that behaviour is testable with a real filesystem instead of a source-text pin.
- **The mobile stall notice's phases, identity and dismissal** — ① phase set: upstream `ConversationRoot` writes `settling`/`hero`/`active` to `[data-phase]`, and the old `['active','engaging']` had a member that never reached the attribute while excluding `settling` (a session opening its history with a visible header); it is now `['settling','active']` with `hero` (no session) still out, and the header-visibility gate keeps the blank-shell case out. ② session identity: the phase node is NOT session-scoped (`main` is keyed by entry identity, so `div.root[data-phase]` is re-rendered in place across a session switch), so the identity is now the displayed `<header>` (the session-scoped `conversation.session.header` subtree is remounted) and neither the clock nor a shown notice carries into the next session. ③ dismissal: a second control ("keep waiting") hides the notice for the current continuous stall instead of forcing a choice between ignoring it and aborting a slow-but-healthy load with a reload, and the suppression ends with that episode (a broken DOM shape OR a hidden page, which also zeroes the clock), so a resumed app onto a still-stalled session is told again. ④ install ownership is reference-counted: a second context used to receive a dead disposer, so disposing the first stopped watching for the second.
- **A stale packaged bundle now fails the gate** — `dist/control-plane` and the gateway bundles inline source logic while the behaviour tests exercise the workspace sources, so an old build stayed green while the packaged app shipped the previous judgement (an older round of the protected-set verifier survived in dist until it was rebuilt by hand). Both guards now fail when an EXISTING bundle lacks the current markers and only build when the artifact is absent; silently rebuilding would have let the check heal the very drift it exists to catch.
- **Gateway audit windows drain again after close, and are capped** — `flushAuditWindows()` drains the debounce windows AGAIN once the HTTP listener is closed: `quiesce()` drains at the admission fence, but a refusal accepted between the fence and the close opened a window nobody ever published, so that record was lost. The window map is also capped (`MAX_AUTH_REJECTION_WINDOWS`): distinct client identities are unbounded, so at the cap the oldest window is published early instead of growing forever.

## [0.3.0] - 2026-09-14

### Added
- **Boot-gap surfacing: a structural gap is no longer a silent empty body (design 05 §4, "degraded presentation")** — a source can "boot successfully" while a whole surface never registers (when the composite first screen's `sidebarRight` is only provided by an optional host-graph row, the fiber stays pending, the conversation view never registers, and the main column keeps nothing but the composer); until now only a console line said so. A gap is now a STRUCTURED fact (kind + payload) with three honest seats: a frame banner (active view only, `role=status`, standing down while the control plane is unreachable), the source row's note (folded into that row's single live region, priority managed-down > gap > transient > baseline), and the connections card plus plugin dialog (the graph status is suppressed while a gap is present, because the graph channel is healthy exactly in the motivating case) — all sharing one retry entry. A verdict arriving before settle is held per boot serial and replayed at settle.

- **GUI acceptance toolbox and process checklist (`pnpm run acceptance:gui`; the checklist is `docs/checklists/gui-acceptance-checklist.md`)** — GUI acceptance previously had no normalized document or reusable procedure: the criteria were scattered across `docs/design/*` (the per-domain gates), `docs/progress/STATUS.md` (open on-device items) and `scripts/perf` (the performance ruler, not functional acceptance), so every round reinvented them and nothing could be regressed. The checklist records **process + pointers** only (four legs: mechanical A/B, visual inspection, packaged state; each row carries a check id and its design/STATUS basis without restating any criterion, so a second ledger that would drift cannot appear); the toolbox adds **zero new dependencies** (Node's built-in `WebSocket`/`fetch`) and splits into a pure-criteria layer `checks.mjs` and a driver layer `probe` (`--live` read-only probing of a running app, installed builds included) / `walkthrough` (CDP walkthrough + screenshots via `--attach`/`--dev`) / `launch` / `run`. Assertions use only the repository's existing DOM contracts (`[data-instance]`, `[data-chamber-section|row]`, `[data-slot]`/`[data-slot-error]`, the settings navigation `dialog nav [class*="navList"] > button`), adding no test hooks and matching no copy; the click allowlist holds only settings navigation items and the first-run close action, so no mutating control can be reached. Registered tolerances (cold-start `clientGraph/graph` 503 = design 09 §3.2, the page's own resubscribe `net::ERR_ABORTED`, upstream cordis startup logs, and the instance face degrading to INFO while an instance is not ready = design 18 §3.4) live in the toolbox README — widening them means changing that document first, never guessing in code.
- **open-in host half forked into an in-instance seed package (design 20 §2.1, §6.3)** — Phase 2's "make both official halves run inside the N-ctx shell" was rejected by three independent pieces of evidence (the `SSH_CONNECTION` directory-selection pin injected into managed instances is shared by three upstream sites, the official client half assumes same-origin absolute paths, and the official row depends on the instance's runtime version), so it became a **fork & supersede**: new `packages/dsh-chamber-seed-open-in/` (a fork of upstream `host/open-in-app`; `catalog`/`resolver`/`icons` are byte-identical pure), with the three deliberate divergences written into the source header — the SSH dormancy gate is deleted, the HTTP routes + self-built connection fence are replaced by the typert Remote `openInApp/{probe,apps,icon,open}` domain carrier, and the Config schema is replaced by constants; the client-side local pool now rides the instance's own generic RPC. This is another bounded exception to the established registry discipline.
- **Settings surface moved to a complete bridge (design 05 §5 revision)** — the settings shell no longer assembles a "shrunken frontend" for the selected source; it renders the `settings.section` ledger of **that source's own boot ctx**, with entries rendered by that ctx's own renderer-bound standard seats — a plugin that is active in the instance's own frontend is equally active here (a real remote WS event stream, live settings-invalidation notifications, real `useSessions`/`useWorkspaces`/`usePanelInfo`/`useResource` seats), so upstream `ui-agent-preset`'s authoring entry matches the instance's local one. Rendering requires both halves of the per-instance-face registry (`settings-source-face.ts`), and the avatar fingerprint must equal the authoritative roster's; while the panel is open that source's shell is kept mounted (**the active view is not switched**) and is torn down on close, while an offline source still shows an unreachable placeholder and triggers no mount.
- **Sidebar: a workspace created on an unmounted source is visible immediately + the open-intent contract** — on-device feedback reported "create a workspace for source B from source A and the row never appears; you have to open B before it refreshes". The fix reports the sidebar's own successful unary `workspace.create` as the single trustworthy fact that "this workspace now exists on that host" (`chamberBridge.reportWorkspaceCreated`) and merges it at the projection's **single convergence point** (no second authority is added — the mounted shell's follow baseline stays authoritative), with three retirement conditions: a real push for the same `workspaceId`/same path, the source leaving the registry, or a 10-minute TTL (armed on three clocks: this create, the authoritative push, and the 30 s unary fallback pull). The same batch hardens the open intent's three gates: the early-open arm keeps reading the intent, verifies the source identity, and corrects the session ownership of the workspace echo (the contract is registered in design 05 §2.2.1).
- **Archive cleanup: force purge, archive-aware worktree removal and a single host-package registry (design 24 §22, design 08)** — archive cleanup gains force/loaded semantics (running and loaded sessions are skipped as a whole tree, and `resolvePlan` reports `skippedLoaded` alongside `skippedRunning`) and shares one run with the registry-global orphan sweep (`readAuthoritativeState` returns both `liveFacts` and `snapshotRecordCount`, and the liveness exclusion set is running ∪ loaded); Git worktree deletion becomes archive-aware; and the three chamber host packages (client-graph / git-worktree / archive-cleanup) unify on **one registry** source, so the gateway's list and the control plane's list are no longer written twice.
- **Upstream touchpoint registry with its freshness gate, and the upstream design-token compliance gate** — new `docs/checklists/upstream-touchpoints.md` (per-file purity registration [pure]/[patch-add]/[patch-mod]/[patch-comment]/[own-divergent]/[own]/[dropped], the dropped table, the deep-import/roster section, the contract mirror table, the artifact registry, and the 8-step maintenance loop per tag) plus `scripts/dev/verify-upstream-touchpoints.mjs` (C1 pure byte-equality / C2 cross-tag replay report / C3 completeness / C4 roster sentinel / C5 anchor scan / C6 excluded-directory existence / C7 seed-domain lockstep / C8 artifact staleness / C9–C10 anchors and live literals / C11–C14 protected-plugin-set gates / C15 hover-card retirement gate), the two maintenance faces mirroring each other and both CI legs running right after the vendor bootstrap; the release workflow's validation leg now proves the release commit passed the full CI on `main` (both the linux `test` and `test-windows` legs) instead of re-running tests on the tag; also `verify:styles` (the upstream design-token compliance gate: hairlines / overlay shadows / namespaces) and a touchpoint self-check section in `PULL_REQUEST_TEMPLATE`.
- **In-session re-proof for the writer-quiescence latch and "clean up and take over" (design 02 §3.4)** — the startup scan opened the latch only on "zero kept and zero errors", and it ran **once at startup only** — so once a record went stale mid-session the latch could never open again, showing up after a hard kill as "the local instance will not start" (`POST /api/connections` permanently 409, start/stop do nothing, only an app restart helps). The two closing reasons are now separated: the **scan verdict** kind gets a bounded re-proof before the start is refused (single-flight + a 2 s cooldown; the common case of an orphan that already exited recovers with no user action at all), while the **write-time termination failure** kind (`onWriterQuiescenceUnknown`) can never be proven by any scan, sticks for the lifetime of this plane and explicitly prompts for a restart; `runReaper` gains a `takeover` mode with per-entry conclusions (`reason`/`takeOverAvailable`) that clear only **this state directory's own** stale/orphan writers — another instance whose owner is still alive is never affected; the connections page offers an explicit "clean up and take over" on that basis.
- **Protected plugin set: the refusal set is derived from the target instance's runtime facts (design 21 §6.11; the revision of decision 19)** — refusing by scope prefix blocks what it should not (official opt-in layers such as `@deepseek-ai/dsh-experimental-*` could not be installed) and failed to block what it was for (a composition being dismantled, a family member shadowed by a cross-generation copy), and it went stale the moment upstream renamed or re-scoped anything. The protected set `P = B₀ ∪ S ∪ F` comes entirely from **verifiable facts about the target instance**: `B₀` = the installation-owned composition snapshot (pinned by gate C12), `S` = the chamber seed registry names (gate C13), and `F` = the **committed** runtime lockfile closure (gate C11: core included, official opt-in and dev/test packages excluded). install and remove are judged in separate phases (remove never judges a version), an official-scope install additionally requires the **exact same generation**, and an underivable `F` falls to a conservative ladder that only ever tightens (a retryable 503 on the gateway side). One judgement covers all three write faces: the control-plane predicate; the gateway's install/materialize/remove routes (including deferred-intent draining, re-judging at execution time and post-install re-verification); and the desktop shell's three local channels (folder/archive pick, renderer-submitted spec, remove) plus ssh apply and in-place materialization (a mixed batch is refused in full — no valid row ever executes around a refused one). The settings "installed" list is driven by **server-projected rows** `{role, protected, owner?}`, so the renderer no longer holds a mirror predicate. Four freshness gates C11–C14 (runtime family closure / profile contract anchors / seed registry structure / manifest three-way mirror plus row types) ride `pnpm run test:upgrade-tools` in both CI legs.
- **Archive cleanup: a resident session no longer comes back into the workspace after its content is deleted (design 24 §4 step 9)** — deleting the content of an archived session that this host process still holds also cleared its archived membership; because the official session list is live-preferred (`listSessions()` = the durable scan ∪ `ctx.sessions.list()`), the host kept serving that row, so the session the user had just deleted re-appeared in the workspace as an ordinary row and looked as if its content had been restored. Now a completed tree whose session is still resident (attached to this process) at deletion time **keeps** its archived membership (the content is still deleted) and the row stays hidden; the archive manager labels those rows "content deleted — clears when this instance restarts" (also carried into the row checkbox's accessible name), and the purge result gains the additive field `residentRetainedRoots` (older clients ignore unknown fields). Once the instance restarts the row is gone and the content-free members are converged by a later run's orphan sweep. Hardened in the same pass: the live-facts read now **refuses loudly** on any drifted shape (silently dropping an entry used to push both the loaded guard and the residency judgement in the unsafe direction), and the batched set removal is preceded by one final live re-check, so a session that attaches inside the write window keeps its membership too (a failed re-check writes nothing at all).

### Changed
- **Chamber surfaces take the official non-density token and geometry values** — several values derived from a "density scale" now take the official non-density values; every chamber popup menu (session/workspace kebab, sort, the Git create dialog's field dropdown, the open-in app menu, the settings server dropdown) runs the primitive's `compact` form (26px / 12px rows), matching the repository-wide "menu density is chamber's own" decision;  the settings server dropdown's radius follows the official 20px.
- **Open-in: catalog and icons answered by the local instance's page-level machine catalog, presentation taken from the official split button** — the app catalog, its icons and the launch description are facts about THE MACHINE, not about the source on screen, so the render shell builds ONE machine catalog against the local instance and injects it into every entry (local and remote alike — a remote source's entries now show the machine's real icons), reusing the local entry's own `/api/i/local` generic RPC; the plugin no longer holds a connection carrier, a missing icon always falls back to the official rounded square, and every chamber-owned bitmap is gone (icon selection collapses into the official rule). The header control's presentation is now the official open-in split button, rule for rule (28px / `border-l4` / r14 box, a 15px mark in the button, the design-system chevron, 18px marks in the menu rows); upstream has no single-entry form, so this entry keeps none either.

- **dsh advanced to the 0.1.5-rc.2 generation (build-time vendor source + bundled runtime, same generation)** — relative to the previous formal release, the source pin, the bundled runtime, the three fork copies (`dsh-client-connection` / `dsh-client-web` / `dsh-api-gateway`) and the installer/gateway `dshAnchorVersion` all sit at `0.1.5-rc.2`. Upstream rewrote the client shell's two-generation slot model over this span (`details`→`rightbar` with the scope moving from session to root; the centre column `conversation`→keyed `main`; a new `sidebar.panellist` axis plus the global `usePanelInfo`/`useResource` seats), so chamber's own layout / sidebar / mobile / settings artifacts were replayed in full while keeping chamber's look and behaviour.
  - **Operator-visible upstream change reaching managed instances through the runtime line**: the base bundle's default model moves `deepseek-v4-flash` → **`deepseek-flash`** (V41 Flash; the catalog goes 3→4 entries, keeping V4 Flash / V4 Pro / V4 Flash Vision Exp); that entry declares `systemPromptUpdate: 'in-history'` (the V4 family declares nothing and behaves as before). Upstream's own caveat: until a gateway enables that id, requests may answer `INVALID_REQUEST` — the settings let you switch back to a V4 entry.
  - **Version-skew registration**: from this generation on, the instance-side document preview depends behaviourally on a same-generation `ui-primitives` (`CodeBlock`'s `contentRef` is its only scroll/line anchor), so an older-generation composite serving a newer instance loses that preview's independent scroll area and line positioning — registered in STATUS under the platform-word deviations.

- **Fixed five same-origin absolute URLs under the N-ctx shell** — the official client assumes it is served by the dsh origin, but under chamber's multi-instance page (N-ctx) the page origin is the control plane, which only proxies `/api/i/<id>/*`; five actions were measurably broken: `ui-chat`'s `/api/file` (broken local Markdown images), `client-file-upload`'s `/api/session/uploadFileBinary` (**composer attachment upload 404**), `ui-deliverables`' `/api/present.host|open` (deliverable-card "open/reveal" 404) and `session-log-export`'s `/api/session.export` (**/export download 404**). The fix is a registered build-time **vendor patch set** (`packages/renderer/scripts/vendor-patches.mjs`, rewriting at exact upstream anchors with zero writes into vendor files, a drifting anchor failing the build; it covers the upload and export clients and falls back to upstream behaviour where the base path is absent under an official layout deployment). **Known boundary**: local and gateway sources work once the control plane injects the cookie; ssh/http dsh targets still have no cookie injection (instance-side 401), an existing authentication-surface item.
- **Chamber package naming unified** — naming is now "directory == package name minus scope": six client plugin packages move `@dsh-chamber/dsh-client-ui-*` → `@dsh-chamber/dsh-chamber-client-ui-*` (directories unchanged) and the three host seed packages become `dsh-chamber-seed-<loader-id>` (`@dsh-chamber/dsh-chamber-seed-client-graph` / `-git-worktree` / `-archive-cleanup`; loader ids, activation-probe domains and release counts unchanged); `dsh-client-ui-mobile` (the client-kind exception), the three fork copies and the infrastructure packages stay put. **Compatibility**: this release's desktop shell syncs host packages under the new names while an in-place gateway installed from v0.2.4 or earlier only accepts the old ones, so such a deployment must update the gateway first; the remote transition rewrites the exact old-name `cordis.patch.yml` lines once (same loader id), so an upgraded remote cannot hard-fail on an id-bound conflict.
- **Connection-recovery hardening (2026-09)** — from the dedicated investigation into "upstream connection management assumes a single local instance, while chamber faces tunnels, slow links and wake events":
  - **Per-source readiness deadline** (new `dsh-client-connection/src/client/recovery-policy.ts`): the chamber page never receives the host-injected `__DSH_CONNECTION_RECOVERY__` global and therefore ran the 15 s hard deadline everywhere; the api-gateway fork now passes 45 s deadline / 5 s warn for ssh/http sources through upstream's supported `connection.start(sinks, config)` seam, while local and unknown transports keep the upstream defaults — a cold SSH tunnel or slow link is no longer cancelled-and-retried forever on handshake timeout.
  - **Wake events bypass the offline gate**: a page frozen across suspend/resume can miss the `online` event entirely and keep reporting offline, which previously also blocked `system-resume`. Wake events now bypass the gate (`reconnect()`'s `immediateRetry` skips the suspension branch and forces exactly one bounded attempt; a genuinely offline host fails fast and re-suspends), while `online`/visibility triggers stay gated; all three share the 10 s debounce.
- **Open-in unification: the single header entry consumes the per-source view model** — local sources absorb the official client:
  - **Local sources (official channel)**: the instance's own official host catalog (`dsh-host-open-in-app`, shipped in the default a2 web bundle) serves the full local application picker through the per-instance proxy `<basePath>/open-in-app/{apps,icon/<id>,open}` — the catalog protocol (`shared/open-in-app-protocol.ts`, byte-lockstep test against the vendor `shared.ts`), real bundle icons (404 falls back to a neutral square), the official `app.*` label table and button copy (merged into the single chamber locale NS), persisted choice (official key `dsh.open-in-app.choice`, in-memory fallback when storage is unavailable) and the busy/error dress (250 ms delayed busy, 2 s error decay) are all absorbed. The desktop main process's VS Code override follows §5.1 ("vscode always goes through the IPC override") plus r6 ("show the union + IPC fallback"): when the main provider reports that id available it wins (vscode rides the IPC path, keeping `vscodeOpenInNewWindow`, the source-generation proof and the deep-link intent push); when it is unavailable the official entry falls back to the instance route.
  - **Remote SSH sources**: the main process's remote-capable entry only (VS Code Remote-SSH; the main process builds the `vscode://vscode-remote` URL); **HTTP/unknown sources**: no entry.
  - **Desktop main-process slimming (redline)**: the `OpenInApp` registry is vscode-only; `OpenInLaunchContext` drops `stat`/`openPath`/`showItemInFolder`, and the finder provider plus `classifyLocalPath`/`invokeOpenPath`/`normalizeOpenPathError`/`shouldRevealDirectoryInsteadOfOpen` are retired. The local launch trust boundary moves from trusted IPC to the instance's own official route (instance trust fence + official resolver whitelist/existence checks); the control plane still has zero execution surface (verbatim proxy + browser-auth cookie injection). The VS Code deep-link semantics, the source-generation proof and the OS-level `dsh-chamber://open-vscode` entry are unchanged.
  - Redline revisions registered in designs 16/20/05 and AGENTS (final design acceptance stays with the user).
- **Upgrade tooling follow-up (2026-09)** — three pieces unrelated to the dsh upgrade version:
  - **Pre-bump pin preflight (new `scripts/dev/preflight-vendor-pin.mjs`, read-only)**: diffs the target tag against the current pin and reports, in one pass, the three fork copies classified as pure/needs-manual-replay/dropped, the vendor seam files chamber deep-imports, upstream package additions and removals, new client rows, and whether the runtime is published to npm; supports `--offline`/`--json`/`--fail-on-replay` (advisory tool: it never mutates the worktree or the submodule HEAD). It turns "discover an upstream shell rewrite only after bumping the pin" into step 0 of the flow: read the list before touching the pin.
  - **Removal guard for the lockfile vendor-record repair script (fix)**: `restore-lockfile-vendor-records.mjs` used to resurrect every importer record pnpm pruned, unconditionally, from HEAD; once upstream removed workspace members (the 4 landlock records at 0.1.5) the script replayed members that no longer exist and the frozen install then failed with "in lockfile, missing link". It now skips (and prints) records whose `vendor/harness-packages/@deepseek-ai/<name>` link is absent, including broken symlinks. New root script `pnpm run test:upgrade-tools` (both script test files) plus a CI step.
  - **Per-transport aggregate staleness thresholds**: the sidebar aggregate's S2 reconnect watchdog used a single 120 s threshold and **covered direct-http sources only**; it is now chosen per source transport — http keeps 120 s (the browser leg has the control plane's 30 s WS ping, but the upstream leg has no application heartbeat and only ~10 min of OS TCP keepalive, so a tight heal is warranted), while **ssh gains a 300 s last-resort arm** (the tunnel already has three independent detectors: the proxy's 30 s/1-miss WS ping, the host mux's 2 s/2-miss heartbeat and the ssh keepalive at 30×3≈90 s; this arm only adds app-level-freeze healing, and its threshold must stay well above the http one so idle healthy tunnels do not pay a baseline replay every two minutes), and local/unknown sources are never touched (`AGGREGATE_RECONNECT_HTTP_STALE_MS`/`AGGREGATE_RECONNECT_SSH_STALE_MS` + `reconnectStalenessMsForTransport`). The unary 30 s pull cadence is unchanged — the watchdog remains an addition to the pull.

- **Settings-source badge retired and ownership judgement moved to npm scope** — the "plugin" source badge on nav rows is retired, returning the row to its upstream shape; ownership is now decided by npm scope.
- **Commit-message language (contribution rules)** — `CONTRIBUTING.md` now states that **commit messages are always English** (both subject and body), that the historical Chinese commits are an existing fact and set no precedent, and that code comments, design documents and PR bodies are not bound by this rule.

### Fixed
- **Session rows resolve their label through the official chain, so "unnamed session" can no longer appear** — the chain is `durable title → cwd basename → session id`, resolved once and carried through both snapshot builders (riding the publish signatures, so a label-only change republishes); the row label, hover card, accessible name, copy text, search rows AND search matching, the todo strip, notification titles and the worktree-removal list all consume that one label, and a historical row whose title the host cannot read is named by its project directory. "Unnamed session" survives only in the declared exceptions (the archive manager's durable-name column, plus the dictionary fallbacks for a row that is not in the projection at all). The same pass makes "+" reopen an existing blank member of the workspace (upstream `connectWorkspace`'s own predicate), so a double click no longer mints a second invisible empty row.
- **Archived content is deletable in every state (design 24 §5)** — the archive manager used to require that the client could name the session it was viewing AND resolve that session's upward subagent chain; both facts are routinely unavailable (no session open, the vendor masking `current` across list gaps, a reclaimed shell, cwd-less cold rows), so the delete controls stayed disabled for exactly the sessions the force path exists for (archived while running, e.g. parked on a question or an approval). One shape now runs in every state: STOP cancels every closure member of the selection (minus the viewed session; bounded fan-out, input-ordered accounting) and can no longer refuse the run; PURGE always carries `force`, and the viewed session travels as a protection set with an honest `skippedProtected` report; an unresolvable current session degrades to a visible hint line instead of disabled controls; and archiving now TERMINATES the archived session and its subagent subtree.
- **Mobile touch tier: right panel, drawer yield and the 30s self-heal gate (design 17 §18.6)** — the panel's forced fullscreen presentation and the drawer yield were gated on the frame's `data-rightbar-collapsed` flag, which means "no retained track" (`cols.rightbar === 0`) and therefore stays false on the phone tier where a shown panel covers the screen; both now anchor on the panel's own `[data-sidebar-right-panel]` (which keeps its box through the close animation), the yield honours both shown signals, the panel carries `env(safe-area-inset-*)` with `box-sizing: border-box` (a content-box panel painted its insets past the viewport on the notch side), the now-inert mode control is hidden in the 768–1023px band only, and the panel subtree contains its overscroll instead of chaining it to the document behind. The composer's 30s stuck-submit clock looked at the busy phase alone and could force-unlock a legitimate lock arriving during a submission (owner block, offline parent, removal); it now also requires the official `aria-disabled` marker to be absent, the decision lives in pure predicates, and both MutationObserver option sets are exported (without `attributeOldValue` that layer is silently dead). The view-tab strip wraps instead of scrolling (a scroll container clips the active bar upstream draws 1px past the tab box), its 44px floor is border-box, and the dockkit strip grows to fit its controls while the chips stay content-box.

- **Purged archived sessions kept resurfacing in the sidebar (design 24 §12).**
  After a purge deleted the content of archived sessions, the official client's
  `SessionManager.summaries` never refreshed (host session events are documented
  no-ops) while the archive-set shrink reached the client immediately through the
  official workspace follow, so the producer pushed "shrunk set + stale rows" and
  the deleted sessions rendered as ordinary rows (clicking one failed with
  `session/not-found`); the 30s unary fallback then cleared them again, producing
  a push-restores / pull-clears flicker. Four layers fix it: (1) producer-side
  **tombstone suppression** (ids that left the authoritative archive set are
  filtered out of the emitted snapshot and the runtime-fact channel until the
  official summaries converge or the id is re-archived); (2) a **verified
  convergence chain** (the official `ctx.sessions.refresh()` is now invoked as a
  METHOD — the previous detached call threw `TypeError` on every attempt and never
  issued a request — with bounded retries for resolved-but-unconverged, rejected
  and hung outcomes, ending in a chamber unary `session.list` authoritative probe
  that releases only ids the server still lists); (3) an App-side **authoritative
  archive-set memory** (shrink baseline when provenance is lost, plus archived-row
  filtering for the degraded unary view; `archiveSetKnown` stays false so the
  archive manager keeps its non-destructive degraded branch); (4) a host-side
  **registry-global orphan sweep** (every purge clears record-less archive-set
  members across the WHOLE set: per-candidate official single-id existence check,
  union of the query/persistence enumerations, empty/collapsed-corpus credibility
  gates, membership-only with zero new content deletion, double-confirmed and
  fail-closed). See design 24 §12 and `docs/progress/STATUS.md`.
- **Mobile web surface (design 17 §18)**: rework and hardening for four
  device-reported problems.
  - **Sticky tooltip ghosts**: an official ui-primitives `Tooltip` tap
    synthesizes mouseenter with no matching mouseleave (sticky hover), so the
    delayed (200-500ms) bubble stays over the send/stop button that was just
    used. The rule is now gated by `(pointer: coarse) and (hover: none)` (wide
    touch devices tap too; attaching a mouse flips hover and stands the rule
    down) and scoped to bubbles that DUPLICATE an accessible name
    (`button[aria-label] + [role="tooltip"][data-side]` — the bubble is the
    trigger's immediate next sibling and carries the component's own
    `data-side`). Four informational bubbles (chat stats line, agent-preset
    card description, trajectory timeline span, ≤620px trajectory kind tag) are
    deliberately kept: their triggers have no accessible equivalent, so hiding
    them would remove a touch user's only readable source; the fifth
    `role="tooltip"` producer (the trajectory turn-rail preview) carries no
    `data-side` and is structurally excluded.
  - **Keyboard compensation hardening**: arming is idempotent per frame element
    — a renderer remount that replaces the AppFrame while the keyboard stays
    open re-stamps the new frame and cleans the old one; arming now requires an
    editable focus (focusin + focusout stamps plus a composer-selection
    fallback for the submit window). The ZOOM POLICY is "serve the composer
    only": a blanket `scale > 1.01` veto would leave the composer behind the
    keyboard for the rest of an iOS focus-zoomed session (the drawer's 13px
    search field is a common trigger), so zoom + a focused
    composer is served while zoom + a non-composer field stays vetoed; the
    focus-zoom trigger itself is removed at the source (the drawer's fields get
    the 16px floor too). The offset quantum is 16px (dead band 8-23px instead
    of 8-55px) and the seat's bottom safe-area padding is zeroed while armed
    (0-34px of double spacing on notched iPhones).
  - **Settings sheet**: the section-switch scroll reset now fires only on a
    section CHIP click (the decision is a pure predicate) and is
    gated on the PHONE tier (a 769-1023px touch tablet keeps the official modal
    geometry and cross-section scroll behavior).
  - **Connection-stability forensics (no fix yet)**: the gateway/control-plane
    shared WS splice teardown now logs one bounded line
    (`WebSocket stream <id> closed (<cause>, <ms>ms)`), making an INSTANCE-side
    mux heartbeat termination distinguishable from a client-initiated reconnect
    (previously only the proxy's own heartbeat was logged; an instance-side
    termination left no trace). The cause is a paren-free token so the whole
    line parses, and a throwing logger can no longer latch the teardown. The
    fix itself still waits on browser-side close-code evidence — findings and
    candidate fixes in `docs/progress/STATUS.md`.

- **Gateway management page accessibility and request boundary** — the confirmation control becomes an **in-page accessible dialog** (no more focus-stealing native confirm), keeping focus trapped while the decision is pending and fixing `aria-busy` attribution; the request boundary is tightened — 400 reasons stay faithful (never rewritten into a generic code), a read-face fence, and auth auditing; the gateway's own pnpm is put on the `PATH` of the `dsh plugin` child process, so plugin tasks no longer fail on a host without pnpm.
- **Git worktree moved onto upstream primitives** — the hand-drawn confirmation control and tag are replaced by the official confirmation dialog and tag primitive (appearance/keyboard/semantics now follow upstream); the risk-confirmation gate now **takes effect on a single gesture** and is locked (consecutive confirmations used to keep asking about the same operation).
- **Desktop: packaging hooks and dev startup** — `beforePack` swaps `node_modules/@dsh-chamber/dsh-runtime` from the workspace link to a dist-only real directory (electron-builder does not follow the link across a Windows junction), but no hook ever restored it — after packaging the workspace lost its type surface, so the root `typecheck` and `typecheck:gateway` cascaded into TS7016 and `build:preload` failed, and `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` could not repair it. The materialize/restore pair is now split into testable functions and restored on exit/SIGINT/SIGTERM/SIGHUP (pollution left by SIGKILL is healed by the next packaging run, and the restore is registered even when the target is already a real directory); `dev` startup no longer rebuilds the renderer layer on every launch.
- **CLI and the dsh runtime probe aligned with the real contract** — `cli`'s runtime gate and read face are corrected to the real contract; `dsh-runtime`'s `commands/execute` probe wire names are aligned and it now surfaces the probe's failure reason, redacts UNC paths, and names the failing probe in its termination verdict (the probe text's reserved words cover legacy method names), so the `gateway`'s shell-upgrade transaction can read the failure reason off it.
- **renderer / sidebar / settings review-round fixes** — failure presentation now goes through the design system with unified shell copy; the open-intent and reveal-gate wiring is hardened (switching sources no longer exposes a blank session the target shell created itself); the sidebar's early-open arm keeps reading the intent, verifies the source identity, and the session ownership of the workspace echo is corrected; settings ownership is now decided by **npm scope** (instead of enumerating repository ids), so server settings rows are no longer mislabeled "plugin", and Escape-to-close is restored for the settings panel.
- **Test portability and CI shape** — `test:host-archive-cleanup`'s uppercase near-miss case cannot hold on a **case-insensitive filesystem** (macOS APFS default / Windows NTFS) at all, so it now branches on a runtime probe; the two test-only ESM loaders used to map specifiers to bare submodule paths or workspace-member paths, making the unit tests depend on **this machine's vendor install shape** (`zustand` failed to resolve on CI with `Cannot find package`), so the cases that "run the real vendor source" now use a contract-shaped store double while real resolution stays guarded by the source lock and `build:renderer`; `purged-tracker`'s negative "exactly one refresh by then" assertion used to race the real `retryMs` timer (`2 !== 1` on CI), so everything now runs on an injected clock and "no leftover timers" is pinned into the assertion; the crashing `test-windows` gate and the undici type landing point in `host-open-in` are fixed in the same round.
- **Mobile card-grid metrics and ARIA description corrections** (continuing the mobile face of design 17 §18).
- **The session-row hover card owns its state machine now (design 06 §7)** — the vendored `HoverCard` decides whether to arm its close grace from the last *committed* `open` (`ui-primitives/HoverCard.tsx:183-188`) while this shell runs every instance's React root on one page: the dwell-to-commit gap measured 502-504ms idle and 501-551ms under load, so a `pointerleave` landing in that window arms nothing, the card mounts with the pointer already gone, and no later event can close it (the observed symptom: a card that stays on screen after a fast sweep). `shared/hover-intent.ts` now makes the **pointer-inside flag** authoritative (re-checked when the dwell fires, unconditional grace on leave), and visibility is read straight from the machine through `useSyncExternalStore` instead of being mirrored into component state (which would still mount a card the machine believes closed when press/disabled and dwell commits interleave); the box, 8px offset, clamping and the copy-on-activation contract are ported from the official atom unchanged. Two **deliberate additions** beyond that atom: one visible row card per document (a page-global slot shared across N-ctx shells — which is also the self-heal path when a leave was never delivered: window blur, a hidden/covered carrier shell, a list moving under a resting pointer) and dismissal on window blur / hidden document.
- **open-in answered an empty catalog in production (design 20 §4.2)** — the page-level machine catalog read only one of the two envelope levels the wire carries: `callUnary` answers the generic-RPC result (`{ok,value}`) whose `value` is the host domain's own `domainResult` carrier, so `parseApps`/`parseIcon` received the carrier instead of its payload — in production `apps()` always answered an empty catalog and every icon was null (the running app showed no Finder and drew the fallback mark for the VS Code entry) Both levels are now read in the module that owns wire parsing (the convention the git and archiveCleanup clients already follow), and a generic-RPC refusal is projected onto the domain carrier's failure arm (a launch reports what the RPC layer said instead of "unrecognizable"). No entry is offered for a gateway source (whose host has no such domain by design — `localOnly`).
- **Chamber's "on/selected" colour is back to the dsh business blue (design 15 §D1②, design 24 §6)** — the settings panel, the connections plugin filter pill, the archive-manager checkbox and the runtime install-progress fill had taken the official neutral `--dsw-alias-brand-primary` (near-black in the light theme, near-white in the dark one), painting "on/selected" black where dsh blue was expected; they now take `--dsw-alias-state-business-primary` (deepseek-500/400), the accent the sidebar's selection/drop indicators and the Git panel slider already use, with one panel-scoped rule (specificity 0,3,0) repainting the primitive's checked track so build order cannot flip it.
- **Restarting into a downloaded update is no longer swallowed by close-to-tray (design 11)** — on macOS `quitAndInstall()` closes every window before quitting, while the default hide-to-tray only lets a close through once `before-quit` has fired (i.e. AFTER the windows closed), so the update's quit leg had its window close eaten: the page vanished, the process, its local dsh and its tunnels lived on forever, and the update was never installed (confirmed on a real machine). The armed restart window now lets the close through and the host fallback forces the quit after 5s; the stall watchdog is now **re-anchored** on the native quit event instead of being cleared — the quit leg cannot be misreported as stalled halfway, and a leg that never completes still ends in the honest stall text with an in-place retry (clearing it left the restart stuck as "in progress" forever).
- **Three mobile layout rules matched no element at all** — the pinned official bundles emit local-first `_<local>_<hash>_<idx>` names while the rules only carried the hash-first suffix arm, so the composer-row single-line rule, the model-trigger truncation and the settings Models row were all silently inert (exactly the "phone tier rendered with desktop geometry" regression that audit existed to fix). The infix arm is now in place, and the model trigger is scoped to the model seat anchor instead of the shared `_trigger_` local name (four modules carry that local name in the composite build, including ContextMeter's 28px ring, whose `flex:none` must not be overridden).
- **One spacing rhythm for the sidebar row action cluster (2026-09 user report)** — the official `Rows .rowActions` 12px fell **inside** a single cluster (the git workspace's revealed branch action is that cluster's leftmost member, sitting in the header's 4px gap), which made the `+` and the kebab read as detached; the whole cluster now uses the 4px rhythm. Same pass: the settings card checkbox nudge is scoped to the head row, the fatal overlay's server-row grouping is corrected, and the settings page no longer shows the local-shape-only row on targets other than this machine (the localOnly projection).
- **The settings "installed" list shows only declared plugins** — the view projected the union of the name registry (dependencies ∪ live bundles ∪ the installation-owned composition ∪ the seed registry), so a profile that declares **nothing** still rendered six rows: the two installation-owned composition members and the four chamber host packages. Neither is a plugin anyone installed, the composition rows were self-contradictory (the official tree is hoisted to `profiles/node_modules`, so their version probe always answered "—"), and the chamber packages already own the "chamber managed components" table (probe state + version + manual re-sync). It now projects **one row per declared dependency**: composition and seed names still classify `role`/`owner` and still feed the protected-set judgement, they just no longer create rows, while a **declared** protected name still renders read-only with its role badge and the write face still refuses both directions. Registered trade-off: a layer that exists only in live `dsh.profile.bundles` no longer gets a row (no in-page remove — CLI only), which can only happen on a hand-edited profile.
- **A gateway-managed instance's runtime switch no longer fails silently** — `shouldInvalidate()` treats a non-null `invalidatedAt` as a permanent invalidation flag and no host ever clears it, while `select()`, `apply()` and `rollback()` all wrote the record by spreading the previous one: a dsh runtime version chosen **after a gateway shell update** was therefore written born-invalidated — its pending was ignored forever, `apply-now` refused it with `no_selection`, and the version-switch intent journal left beside the still-stamped record was classified `selection-corrupt`. `select()` and `rollback()` now consume the active stamp (null it and fold the time/reason into the `lastInvalidated*` history the F4 echo keeps), and `apply()` gains the same invalidation gate `apply-now` already had — an invalidated selection is refused honestly with 409 `no_selection` instead of returning a pending the core silently drops. An instance already stranded recovers by re-selecting once (previously only deleting the runtime record helped). The desktop host is unaffected: its controller writes a fresh literal record on install and on cached rollback.


## [0.3.0-beta.4] - 2026-09-13
### Added
- **Protected plugin set: the refusal set is derived from runtime facts instead of guessed from scope prefixes (design 21 §6.11; the revision of decision 19)** — the old rule blocked what it should not (official opt-in layers such as `@deepseek-ai/dsh-experimental-*` could not be installed) and failed to block what it was for (a composition being dismantled, a family member shadowed by a cross-generation copy), and it went stale the moment upstream renamed or re-scoped anything. The protected set `P = B₀ ∪ S ∪ F` now comes entirely from **verifiable facts about the target instance**: `B₀` = the installation-owned composition snapshot (pinned by gate C12), `S` = the chamber seed registry names (gate C13), and `F` = the **committed** runtime lockfile closure (gate C11: core included, official opt-in and dev/test packages excluded). install and remove are judged in separate phases (remove never judges a version), an official-scope install additionally requires the **exact same generation**, and an underivable `F` falls to a conservative ladder that only ever tightens (a retryable 503 on the gateway side). One judgement covers all three write faces: the control-plane predicate; the gateway's install/materialize/remove routes (including deferred-intent draining, re-judging at execution time and post-install re-verification); and the desktop shell's three local channels (folder/archive pick, renderer-submitted spec, remove) plus ssh apply and in-place materialization (a mixed batch is refused in full — no valid row ever executes around a refused one). The settings "installed" list is driven by **server-projected rows** `{role, protected, owner?}`, so the renderer no longer holds a mirror predicate. Four freshness gates C11–C14 (runtime family closure / profile contract anchors / seed registry structure / manifest three-way mirror plus row types) ride `pnpm run test:upgrade-tools` in both CI legs.
- **Archive cleanup: a resident session no longer comes back into the workspace after its content is deleted (design 24 §4 step 9)** — deleting the content of an archived session that this host process still holds also cleared its archived membership; because the official session list is live-preferred (`listSessions()` = the durable scan ∪ `ctx.sessions.list()`), the host kept serving that row, so the session the user had just deleted re-appeared in the workspace as an ordinary row and looked as if its content had been restored. Now a completed tree whose session is still resident (attached to this process) at deletion time **keeps** its archived membership (the content is still deleted) and the row stays hidden; the archive manager labels those rows "content deleted — clears when this instance restarts" (also carried into the row checkbox's accessible name), and the purge result gains the additive field `residentRetainedRoots` (older clients ignore unknown fields). Once the instance restarts the row is gone and the content-free members are converged by a later run's orphan sweep. Hardened in the same pass: the live-facts read now **refuses loudly** on any drifted shape (silently dropping an entry used to push both the loaded guard and the residency judgement in the unsafe direction), and the batched set removal is preceded by one final live re-check, so a session that attaches inside the write window keeps its membership too (a failed re-check writes nothing at all).
### Fixed
- **Restarting into a downloaded update is no longer swallowed by close-to-tray (design 11)** — on macOS `quitAndInstall()` closes every window before quitting, while the default hide-to-tray only lets a close through once `before-quit` has fired (i.e. AFTER the windows closed), so the update's quit leg had its window close eaten: the page vanished, the process, its local dsh and its tunnels lived on forever, and the update was never installed (confirmed on a real machine). The armed restart window now lets the close through and the host fallback forces the quit after 5s; the stall watchdog is now **re-anchored** on the native quit event instead of being cleared — the quit leg cannot be misreported as stalled halfway, and a leg that never completes still ends in the honest stall text with an in-place retry (clearing it left the restart stuck as "in progress" forever).
- **Sidebar hover cards no longer strand (design 06 §7)** — the vendor `HoverCard` arms its grace close against the **last committed `open`**: a pointerleave landing between the dwell timer firing and React's commit arms nothing, the card then mounts with the pointer already gone, and **no further event can ever close it** (CDP reproduction: 7 stranded cards in 45 samples). This repository now uses its own `RowHoverCard` + `hover-intent` state machine to eliminate that race and closes the remaining stranding paths (view hidden, row unmounted, off-screen, press-to-dismiss); on coarse pointers a tap on the official hover card itself dismisses it. A new gate C15 locks **both** sides of the race and its two time constants against the frozen pin (the retirement condition is upstream fixing that race; a shape drift hard-fails and forces the decision).
- **Three mobile layout rules matched no element at all** — the pinned official bundles emit local-first `_<local>_<hash>_<idx>` names while the rules only carried the hash-first suffix arm, so the composer-row single-line rule, the model-trigger truncation and the settings Models row were all silently inert (exactly the "phone tier rendered with desktop geometry" regression that audit existed to fix). The infix arm is now in place, and the model trigger is scoped to the model seat anchor instead of the shared `_trigger_` local name (four modules carry that local name in the composite build, including ContextMeter's 28px ring, whose `flex:none` must not be overridden).
- **One spacing rhythm for the sidebar row action cluster (2026-09 user report)** — the official `Rows .rowActions` 12px fell **inside** a single cluster (the git workspace's revealed branch action is that cluster's leftmost member, sitting in the header's 4px gap), which made the `+` and the kebab read as detached; the whole cluster now uses the 4px rhythm. Same pass: the settings card checkbox nudge is scoped to the head row, the fatal overlay's server-row grouping is corrected, and the settings page no longer shows the local-shape-only row on targets other than this machine (the localOnly projection).
### Changed
- **The upstream-touchpoint registry now spans C1–C15** (new C11–C14 gates for the protected set and C15 for the hover-card retirement; the registry and `scripts/dev/verify-upstream-touchpoints.mjs` are mirrored and both CI passes run them); the release workflow's validation leg now **proves the release commit already passed full CI on `main`** (both the linux `test` and the `test-windows` leg) instead of re-running the tests on the tag.

## [0.3.0-beta.3] - 2026-09-13
### Added
- **Boot-gap surfacing: a structural gap is no longer a silent empty body (design 05 §4, "degraded presentation")** — a source can "boot successfully" while a whole surface never registers (when the composite first screen's `sidebarRight` is only provided by an optional host-graph row, the fiber stays pending, the conversation view never registers, and the main column keeps nothing but the composer); until now only a console line said so. A gap is now a STRUCTURED fact (kind + payload) with three honest seats: a frame banner (active view only, `role=status`, standing down while the control plane is unreachable), the source row's note (folded into that row's single live region, priority managed-down > gap > transient > baseline), and the connections card plus plugin dialog (the graph status is suppressed while a gap is present, because the graph channel is healthy exactly in the motivating case) — all sharing one retry entry. A verdict arriving before settle is held per boot serial and replayed at settle.

- **GUI acceptance toolbox and process checklist (`pnpm run acceptance:gui`; the checklist is `docs/checklists/gui-acceptance-checklist.md`)** — GUI acceptance previously had no normalized document or reusable procedure: the criteria were scattered across `docs/design/*` (the per-domain gates), `docs/progress/STATUS.md` (open on-device items) and `scripts/perf` (the performance ruler, not functional acceptance), so every round reinvented them and nothing could be regressed. The checklist records **process + pointers** only (four legs: mechanical A/B, visual inspection, packaged state; each row carries a check id and its design/STATUS basis without restating any criterion, so a second ledger that would drift cannot appear); the toolbox adds **zero new dependencies** (Node's built-in `WebSocket`/`fetch`) and splits into a pure-criteria layer `checks.mjs` (16 unit tests joining the linux test job) and a driver layer `probe` (`--live` read-only probing of a running app, installed builds included) / `walkthrough` (CDP walkthrough + screenshots via `--attach`/`--dev`) / `launch` / `run`. Assertions use only the repository's existing DOM contracts (`[data-instance]`, `[data-chamber-section|row]`, `[data-slot]`/`[data-slot-error]`, the settings navigation `dialog nav [class*="navList"] > button`), adding no test hooks and matching no copy; the click allowlist holds only settings navigation items and the first-run close action, so no mutating control can be reached. Registered tolerances (cold-start `clientGraph/graph` 503 = design 09 §3.2, the page's own resubscribe `net::ERR_ABORTED`, upstream cordis startup logs, and the instance face degrading to INFO while an instance is not ready = design 18 §3.4) live in the toolbox README — widening them means changing that document first, never guessing in code.
- **open-in host half forked into an in-instance seed package (design 20 §2.1, §6.3)** — Phase 2's "make both official halves run inside the N-ctx shell" was rejected by three independent pieces of evidence (the `SSH_CONNECTION` directory-selection pin injected into managed instances is shared by three upstream sites, the official client half assumes same-origin absolute paths, and the official row depends on the instance's runtime version), so it became a **fork & supersede**: new `packages/dsh-chamber-seed-open-in/` (a fork of upstream `host/open-in-app`; `catalog`/`resolver`/`icons` are byte-identical pure), with the three deliberate divergences written into the source header — the SSH dormancy gate is deleted, the HTTP routes + self-built connection fence are replaced by the typert Remote `openInApp/{probe,apps,icon,open}` domain carrier, and the Config schema is replaced by constants; the client-side local pool now rides the instance's own generic RPC. This is another bounded exception to the established registry discipline.
- **Settings surface moved to a complete bridge (design 05 §5 revision)** — the settings shell no longer assembles a "shrunken frontend" for the selected source; it renders the `settings.section` ledger of **that source's own boot ctx**, with entries rendered by that ctx's own renderer-bound standard seats — a plugin that is active in the instance's own frontend is equally active here (a real remote WS event stream, live settings-invalidation notifications, real `useSessions`/`useWorkspaces`/`usePanelInfo`/`useResource` seats), so upstream `ui-agent-preset`'s authoring entry matches the instance's local one. Rendering requires both halves of the per-instance-face registry (`settings-source-face.ts`), and the avatar fingerprint must equal the authoritative roster's; while the panel is open that source's shell is kept mounted (**the active view is not switched**) and is torn down on close, while an offline source still shows an unreachable placeholder and triggers no mount.
- **Sidebar: a workspace created on an unmounted source is visible immediately + the open-intent contract** — on-device feedback reported "create a workspace for source B from source A and the row never appears; you have to open B before it refreshes". The fix reports the sidebar's own successful unary `workspace.create` as the single trustworthy fact that "this workspace now exists on that host" (`chamberBridge.reportWorkspaceCreated`) and merges it at the projection's **single convergence point** (no second authority is added — the mounted shell's follow baseline stays authoritative), with three retirement conditions: a real push for the same `workspaceId`/same path, the source leaving the registry, or a 10-minute TTL (armed on three clocks: this create, the authoritative push, and the 30 s unary fallback pull). The same batch hardens the open intent's three gates: the early-open arm keeps reading the intent, verifies the source identity, and corrects the session ownership of the workspace echo (the contract is registered in design 05 §2.2.1).
- **Archive cleanup: force purge, archive-aware worktree removal and a single host-package registry (design 24 §22, design 08)** — archive cleanup gains force/loaded semantics (running and loaded sessions are skipped as a whole tree, and `resolvePlan` reports `skippedLoaded` alongside `skippedRunning`) and shares one run with the registry-global orphan sweep (`readAuthoritativeState` returns both `liveFacts` and `snapshotRecordCount`, and the liveness exclusion set is running ∪ loaded); Git worktree deletion becomes archive-aware; and the three chamber host packages (client-graph / git-worktree / archive-cleanup) unify on **one registry** source, so the gateway's list and the control plane's list are no longer written twice.
- **Upstream touchpoint registry with its freshness gate, and the upstream design-token compliance gate** — new `docs/checklists/upstream-touchpoints.md` (per-file purity registration [pure]/[patch-add]/[patch-mod]/[patch-comment]/[own-divergent]/[own]/[dropped], the dropped table, the deep-import/roster section, the contract mirror table, the artifact registry, and the 8-step maintenance loop per tag) plus `scripts/dev/verify-upstream-touchpoints.mjs` (C1 pure byte-equality / C2 cross-tag replay report / C3 completeness / C4 roster sentinel / C5 anchor scan / C6 excluded-directory existence / C7 seed-domain lockstep / C8 artifact staleness / C9–C10 anchors and live literals), the two maintenance faces mirroring each other and both CI legs running right after the vendor bootstrap; also `verify:styles` (the upstream design-token compliance gate: hairlines / overlay shadows / namespaces) and a touchpoint self-check section in `PULL_REQUEST_TEMPLATE`.
- **In-session re-proof for the writer-quiescence latch and "clean up and take over" (design 02 §3.4)** — the startup scan opened the latch only on "zero kept and zero errors", and it ran **once at startup only** — so once a record went stale mid-session the latch could never open again, showing up after a hard kill as "the local instance will not start" (`POST /api/connections` permanently 409, start/stop do nothing, only an app restart helps). The two closing reasons are now separated: the **scan verdict** kind gets a bounded re-proof before the start is refused (single-flight + a 2 s cooldown; the common case of an orphan that already exited recovers with no user action at all), while the **write-time termination failure** kind (`onWriterQuiescenceUnknown`) can never be proven by any scan, sticks for the lifetime of this plane and explicitly prompts for a restart; `runReaper` gains a `takeover` mode with per-entry conclusions (`reason`/`takeOverAvailable`) that clear only **this state directory's own** stale/orphan writers — another instance whose owner is still alive is never affected; the connections page offers an explicit "clean up and take over" on that basis.

### Changed
- **Chamber surfaces take the official non-density token and geometry values** — several values the previous round derived from a "density scale" return to the official non-density values; every chamber popup menu (session/workspace kebab, sort, the Git create dialog's field dropdown, the open-in app menu, the settings server dropdown) runs the primitive's `compact` form (26px / 12px rows), matching the repository-wide "menu density is chamber's own" decision; icon buttons keep their visual box and grow a 24px hit area; the settings server dropdown's radius follows the official 20px.
- **Open-in: catalog and icons answered by the local instance's page-level machine catalog, presentation taken from the official split button** — the app catalog, its icons and the launch description are facts about THE MACHINE, not about the source on screen, so the render shell builds ONE machine catalog against the local instance and injects it into every entry (local and remote alike — a remote source's entries now show the machine's real icons), reusing the local entry's own `/api/i/local` generic RPC; the plugin no longer holds a connection carrier, a missing icon always falls back to the official rounded square, and every chamber-owned bitmap is gone (icon selection collapses into the official rule). The header control's presentation is now the official open-in split button, rule for rule (28px / `border-l4` / r14 box, a 15px mark in the button, the design-system chevron, 18px marks in the menu rows); upstream has no single-entry form, so this entry keeps none either.

- **dsh advanced to the 0.1.5-rc.2 generation (build-time vendor source + bundled runtime, same generation)** — relative to the previous formal release, the source pin, the bundled runtime, the three fork copies (`dsh-client-connection` / `dsh-client-web` / `dsh-api-gateway`) and the installer/gateway `dshAnchorVersion` all sit at `0.1.5-rc.2`. Upstream rewrote the client shell's two-generation slot model over this span (`details`→`rightbar` with the scope moving from session to root; the centre column `conversation`→keyed `main`; a new `sidebar.panellist` axis plus the global `usePanelInfo`/`useResource` seats), so chamber's own layout / sidebar / mobile / settings artifacts were replayed in full while keeping chamber's look and behaviour.
  - **Operator-visible upstream change reaching managed instances through the runtime line**: the base bundle's default model moves `deepseek-v4-flash` → **`deepseek-flash`** (V41 Flash; the catalog goes 3→4 entries, keeping V4 Flash / V4 Pro / V4 Flash Vision Exp); that entry declares `systemPromptUpdate: 'in-history'` (the V4 family declares nothing and behaves as before). Upstream's own caveat: until a gateway enables that id, requests may answer `INVALID_REQUEST` — the settings let you switch back to a V4 entry.
  - **Version-skew registration**: from this generation on, the instance-side document preview depends behaviourally on a same-generation `ui-primitives` (`CodeBlock`'s `contentRef` is its only scroll/line anchor), so an older-generation composite serving a newer instance loses that preview's independent scroll area and line positioning — registered in STATUS under the platform-word deviations.

- **Fixed five same-origin absolute URLs under the N-ctx shell** — the official client assumes it is served by the dsh origin, but under chamber's multi-instance page (N-ctx) the page origin is the control plane, which only proxies `/api/i/<id>/*`; five actions were measurably broken: `ui-chat`'s `/api/file` (broken local Markdown images), `client-file-upload`'s `/api/session/uploadFileBinary` (**composer attachment upload 404**), `ui-deliverables`' `/api/present.host|open` (deliverable-card "open/reveal" 404) and `session-log-export`'s `/api/session.export` (**/export download 404**). The fix is a registered build-time **vendor patch set** (`packages/renderer/scripts/vendor-patches.mjs`, rewriting at exact upstream anchors with zero writes into vendor files, a drifting anchor failing the build; it covers the upload and export clients and falls back to upstream behaviour where the base path is absent under an official layout deployment). **Known boundary**: local and gateway sources work once the control plane injects the cookie; ssh/http dsh targets still have no cookie injection (instance-side 401), an existing authentication-surface item.
- **Chamber package naming unified** — naming is now "directory == package name minus scope": six client plugin packages move `@dsh-chamber/dsh-client-ui-*` → `@dsh-chamber/dsh-chamber-client-ui-*` (directories unchanged) and the three host seed packages become `dsh-chamber-seed-<loader-id>` (`@dsh-chamber/dsh-chamber-seed-client-graph` / `-git-worktree` / `-archive-cleanup`; loader ids, activation-probe domains and release counts unchanged); `dsh-client-ui-mobile` (the client-kind exception), the three fork copies and the infrastructure packages stay put. **Compatibility**: this release's desktop shell syncs host packages under the new names while an in-place gateway installed from v0.2.4 or earlier only accepts the old ones, so such a deployment must update the gateway first; the remote transition rewrites the exact old-name `cordis.patch.yml` lines once (same loader id), so an upgraded remote cannot hard-fail on an id-bound conflict.
- **Connection-recovery hardening (2026-09)** — from the dedicated investigation into "upstream connection management assumes a single local instance, while chamber faces tunnels, slow links and wake events":
  - **Per-source readiness deadline** (new `dsh-client-connection/src/client/recovery-policy.ts`): the chamber page never receives the host-injected `__DSH_CONNECTION_RECOVERY__` global and therefore ran the 15 s hard deadline everywhere; the api-gateway fork now passes 45 s deadline / 5 s warn for ssh/http sources through upstream's supported `connection.start(sinks, config)` seam, while local and unknown transports keep the upstream defaults — a cold SSH tunnel or slow link is no longer cancelled-and-retried forever on handshake timeout.
  - **Wake events bypass the offline gate**: a page frozen across suspend/resume can miss the `online` event entirely and keep reporting offline, which previously also blocked `system-resume`. Wake events now bypass the gate (`reconnect()`'s `immediateRetry` skips the suspension branch and forces exactly one bounded attempt; a genuinely offline host fails fast and re-suspends), while `online`/visibility triggers stay gated; all three share the 10 s debounce.
- **Open-in unification: the single header entry consumes the per-source view model** — local sources absorb the official client:
  - **Local sources (official channel)**: the instance's own official host catalog (`dsh-host-open-in-app`, shipped in the default a2 web bundle) serves the full local application picker through the per-instance proxy `<basePath>/open-in-app/{apps,icon/<id>,open}` — the catalog protocol (`shared/open-in-app-protocol.ts`, byte-lockstep test against the vendor `shared.ts`), real bundle icons (404 falls back to a neutral square), the official `app.*` label table and button copy (merged into the single chamber locale NS), persisted choice (official key `dsh.open-in-app.choice`, in-memory fallback when storage is unavailable) and the busy/error dress (250 ms delayed busy, 2 s error decay) are all absorbed. The desktop main process's VS Code override follows §5.1 ("vscode always goes through the IPC override") plus r6 ("show the union + IPC fallback"): when the main provider reports that id available it wins (vscode rides the IPC path, keeping `vscodeOpenInNewWindow`, the source-generation proof and the deep-link intent push); when it is unavailable the official entry falls back to the instance route.
  - **Remote SSH sources**: the main process's remote-capable entry only (VS Code Remote-SSH; the main process builds the `vscode://vscode-remote` URL); **HTTP/unknown sources**: no entry.
  - **Desktop main-process slimming (redline)**: the `OpenInApp` registry is vscode-only; `OpenInLaunchContext` drops `stat`/`openPath`/`showItemInFolder`, and the finder provider plus `classifyLocalPath`/`invokeOpenPath`/`normalizeOpenPathError`/`shouldRevealDirectoryInsteadOfOpen` are retired. The local launch trust boundary moves from trusted IPC to the instance's own official route (instance trust fence + official resolver whitelist/existence checks); the control plane still has zero execution surface (verbatim proxy + browser-auth cookie injection). The VS Code deep-link semantics, the source-generation proof and the OS-level `dsh-chamber://open-vscode` entry are unchanged.
  - Redline revisions registered in designs 16/20/05 and AGENTS (final design acceptance stays with the user).
- **Upgrade tooling follow-up (2026-09)** — three pieces unrelated to the dsh upgrade version, all validated on the current pin:
  - **Pre-bump pin preflight (new `scripts/dev/preflight-vendor-pin.mjs`, read-only)**: diffs the target tag against the current pin and reports, in one pass, the three fork copies classified as pure/needs-manual-replay/dropped, the vendor seam files chamber deep-imports, upstream package additions and removals, new client rows, and whether the runtime is published to npm; supports `--offline`/`--json`/`--fail-on-replay` (advisory tool: it never mutates the worktree or the submodule HEAD). It turns "discover an upstream shell rewrite only after bumping the pin" into step 0 of the flow: read the list before touching the pin.
  - **Removal guard for the lockfile vendor-record repair script (fix)**: `restore-lockfile-vendor-records.mjs` used to resurrect every importer record pnpm pruned, unconditionally, from HEAD; once upstream removed workspace members (the 4 landlock records at 0.1.5) the script replayed members that no longer exist and the frozen install then failed with "in lockfile, missing link". It now skips (and prints) records whose `vendor/harness-packages/@deepseek-ai/<name>` link is absent, including broken symlinks. New root script `pnpm run test:upgrade-tools` (both script test files) plus a CI step.
  - **Per-transport aggregate staleness thresholds**: the sidebar aggregate's S2 reconnect watchdog used a single 120 s threshold and **covered direct-http sources only**; it is now chosen per source transport — http keeps 120 s (the browser leg has the control plane's 30 s WS ping, but the upstream leg has no application heartbeat and only ~10 min of OS TCP keepalive, so a tight heal is warranted), while **ssh gains a 300 s last-resort arm** (the tunnel already has three independent detectors: the proxy's 30 s/1-miss WS ping, the host mux's 2 s/2-miss heartbeat and the ssh keepalive at 30×3≈90 s; this arm only adds app-level-freeze healing, and its threshold must stay well above the http one so idle healthy tunnels do not pay a baseline replay every two minutes), and local/unknown sources are never touched (`AGGREGATE_RECONNECT_HTTP_STALE_MS`/`AGGREGATE_RECONNECT_SSH_STALE_MS` + `reconnectStalenessMsForTransport`). The unary 30 s pull cadence is unchanged — the watchdog remains an addition to the pull. Two new unit tests (http/ssh thresholds, local/unknown skipped).

- **Settings-source badge retired and ownership judgement moved to npm scope** — the "plugin" source badge on nav rows is retired, returning the row to its upstream shape; ownership is now decided by npm scope.
- **Commit-message language (contribution rules)** — `CONTRIBUTING.md` now states that **commit messages are always English** (both subject and body), that the historical Chinese commits are an existing fact and set no precedent, and that code comments, design documents and PR bodies are not bound by this rule.

### Fixed
- **Session rows resolve their label through the official chain, so "unnamed session" can no longer appear** — the chain is `durable title → cwd basename → session id`, resolved once and carried through both snapshot builders (riding the publish signatures, so a label-only change republishes); the row label, hover card, accessible name, copy text, search rows AND search matching, the todo strip, notification titles and the worktree-removal list all consume that one label, and a historical row whose title the host cannot read is named by its project directory. "Unnamed session" survives only in the declared exceptions (the archive manager's durable-name column, plus the dictionary fallbacks for a row that is not in the projection at all). The same pass makes "+" reopen an existing blank member of the workspace (upstream `connectWorkspace`'s own predicate), so a double click no longer mints a second invisible empty row.
- **Archived content is deletable in every state (design 24 §5)** — the archive manager used to require that the client could name the session it was viewing AND resolve that session's upward subagent chain; both facts are routinely unavailable (no session open, the vendor masking `current` across list gaps, a reclaimed shell, cwd-less cold rows), so the delete controls stayed disabled for exactly the sessions the force path exists for (archived while running, e.g. parked on a question or an approval). One shape now runs in every state: STOP cancels every closure member of the selection (minus the viewed session; bounded fan-out, input-ordered accounting) and can no longer refuse the run; PURGE always carries `force`, and the viewed session travels as a protection set with an honest `skippedProtected` report; an unresolvable current session degrades to a visible hint line instead of disabled controls; and archiving now TERMINATES the archived session and its subagent subtree.
- **Mobile touch tier: right panel, drawer yield and the 30s self-heal gate (design 17 §18.6)** — the panel's forced fullscreen presentation and the drawer yield were gated on the frame's `data-rightbar-collapsed` flag, which means "no retained track" (`cols.rightbar === 0`) and therefore stays false on the phone tier where a shown panel covers the screen; both now anchor on the panel's own `[data-sidebar-right-panel]` (which keeps its box through the close animation), the yield honours both shown signals, the panel carries `env(safe-area-inset-*)` with `box-sizing: border-box` (a content-box panel painted its insets past the viewport on the notch side), the now-inert mode control is hidden in the 768–1023px band only, and the panel subtree contains its overscroll instead of chaining it to the document behind. The composer's 30s stuck-submit clock looked at the busy phase alone and could force-unlock a legitimate lock arriving during a submission (owner block, offline parent, removal); it now also requires the official `aria-disabled` marker to be absent, the decision lives in pure predicates, and both MutationObserver option sets are exported (without `attributeOldValue` that layer is silently dead). The view-tab strip wraps instead of scrolling (a scroll container clips the active bar upstream draws 1px past the tab box), its 44px floor is border-box, and the dockkit strip grows to fit its controls while the chips stay content-box.

- **Purged archived sessions kept resurfacing in the sidebar (design 24 §12).**
